The over-budget shell for Branch B is the **FP-045 queue engine with page-batches as cursor units** (page numbers are the cursor; one slice = N pages; the queue serializes slices the same way it serializes per-symbol work). Cross-references row updated accordingly: FP-043 entry removed; FP-045 is the over-budget shell. The Resolution-Shape paragraph also stands corrected — read "FP-045 queue engine with page-cursor units" everywhere the original said "FP-043 chunked-coordinator/worker template". |
| **Problem** | Signal #8 (`news_sentiment_7d`) is the next planned Phase-2 non-news/non-catalyst signal — registry row seeded `planned` at MIG-076 (`supabase/migrations/20260608152448_71f5dd70-d2ba-4380-b8f8-7d97af1196ee.sql`, `signal_id='news_sentiment_7d'`, `signal_number=8`, `criticality=non_critical`, `planned_phase='Phase 2.5'`, `job_registry_id=NULL`). CROSSWIND §4.4.8 (`docs/04-modules/longshort/design-source/CROSSWIND_SPEC.md:522`, quoted verbatim below) specifies: `signal_N = Σ_{A in 7d} provider_sentiment(A) × source_weight(A) × exp(−age_hours/24)` with provider_sentiment ∈ [−1,+1], source_weights ∈ {1.0, 0.7, 0.4, 0.1} per a 4-tier publisher classification, press releases excluded, provider pre-classified sentiment (Polygon/Benzinga per spec) with FinBERT local fallback (LLM v2-deferred), within-sector GICS z-score, 24h exponential half-life, multi-ticker articles split per the §4.4.8 primary-vs-equal-split rule. The Phase-0 probe maps spec→vendor shape and surfaces the v1 conscious-approximation set for DEC-056 ratification.<br><br>**§4.4.8 verbatim (CROSSWIND_SPEC.md:522-541):** `For each article A about name N in trailing 7 days: sentiment_A = provider_sentiment(A) // -1 to +1; source_weight(A) = 1.0 (tier-1: Reuters, Bloomberg, WSJ, FT, Dow Jones) / 0.7 (tier-2: CNBC, Forbes, Barron's, NYT business) / 0.4 (tier-3: Yahoo Finance, MarketWatch, aggregators) / 0.1 (tier-4: blogs, regional, low-quality); age_weight(A) = exp(-age_in_hours / 24); raw_signal_N = sum(sentiment_A × source_weight(A) × age_weight(A))`. Source filtering: "Wire + major newspapers + business publications. Press releases excluded." Multi-ticker: "Full sentiment to primary subject ticker; partial weight (divided by mentioned tickers) when no primary tag." |
| **Phase-0 probe evidence (live Polygon, 2026-06-11, 25 calls total — well under 40-call budget)** | **Entitlement:** `GET /v2/reference/news?ticker=AAPL&limit=10` → HTTP 200, real data, 429 ms wall, 10 results, `next_url` present. NOT plan-gated at our current Polygon entitlement (key already in env as the bars vendor; `Authorization: Bearer` accepted via Supabase secret — local dev-sandbox `POLYGON_API_KEY` returns 401 "Unknown API Key" but that is a sandbox-key issue, not endpoint gating; the probe ran from the deployed `probe-fp048-news` edge function reading the real Supabase secret). **Filter-honesty pre-flight (verifyFilterHonored pattern; mandatory per `_pattern-vendor-fetcher-filter-honesty.md`):** `ticker=ZZZZZZZZ&limit=10` → 200 / `count=0` ✓; `published_utc.gte=2099-01-01&published_utc.lte=2099-01-02&limit=10` → 200 / `count=0` ✓ — BOTH axes pass; the documented filters are honored. **Per-item shape (verified fields on AAPL probe):** `id`, `title`, `author`, `description`, `publisher.{name, homepage_url, favicon_url, logo_url}`, `published_utc` (ISO-8601 to-the-minute), `tickers[]`, `keywords[]`, `insights[]` of `{ticker, sentiment, sentiment_reasoning}` — **insights[] carries per-(article,ticker) sentiment**: in the AAPL=2026-06-11 WWDC item, AAPL→neutral while GOOG/GOOGL→positive on the same article (the multi-ticker per-ticker shape the spec needs is present). **Sentiment value domain:** observed values across the 1000-item global probe are `{positive, neutral, negative, mixed}` — categorical strings, NOT numeric ∈ [−1,+1]; mapping to numeric is a DEC-056 binding and "mixed" is a 4th case the spec does not enumerate. **insights presence rate:** 1000/1000 articles in the trailing global feed have non-empty `insights[]` → **100 % presence at our entitlement** — the FinBERT local fallback (§4.4.8) is unnecessary at v1 and is deferred to v2 as a named deferral, NOT silently dropped. **Publisher distribution (global feed `?limit=1000&order=desc&sort=published_utc`, single page, 6.3 s wall):** `GlobeNewswire Inc.` 371 (37.1 %), `The Motley Fool` 307 (30.7 %), `Benzinga` 255 (25.5 %), `Investing.com` 67 (6.7 %). **CRITICAL FINDING — narrow publisher pool:** at our Polygon entitlement the news pool consists of FOUR publishers. NONE of the §4.4.8 tier-1 wires (Reuters, Bloomberg, WSJ, FT, Dow Jones) and NONE of the tier-2 names (CNBC, Forbes, Barron's, NYT) appear in the most-recent 1000 items; a 7-day window probe returned the same four-publisher set. **Press-release exclusion is straightforward:** GlobeNewswire is a press-release wire and the publisher field cleanly identifies it (and PR Newswire / BusinessWire / Accesswire if/when they appear) — exclusion is by `publisher.name`-against-deny-list, NOT by content classification. Excluding GlobeNewswire removes 37 % of feed volume; what remains (Motley Fool / Benzinga / Investing.com) is tier-2-through-tier-4 only under the spec table. **Pagination:** `next_url` returned on every non-empty page; `limit=1000` is the per-page max. **Global feed depth:** the 7-day-window probe returned a full `limit=1000` page with `next_url_present=true` — global feed shape is supported (no ticker filter required). **Coverage sample across cap strata (20 names trailing 7d via per-symbol `ticker={t}&published_utc.gte={d-7}&limit=1000`):** NVDA 204, MSFT 92, AAPL 63, JPM 12, KO 12, BA 10, COST 9, GE 8, V 7, WMT 7, CAT 7, PFE 7, XOM 6, HD 6, DIS 5, NKE 4, UNH 3, TMUS 3, ABBV 2, MA 1. **All 20 names have ≥1 article in 7d; insights present on 100 % of returned articles.** Median ~7 articles/7d for non-mega names; mega-caps have 60–200+. Implication: the 7-day window will produce a non-empty panel for the vast majority of S&P 900 universe names; sparse-coverage small-caps emit the typed skip `no_articles_in_window` (added to §(e) of DEC-056 below). **Calls consumed:** 1 (entitlement) + 2 (publishers global, two windows) + 20 (coverage) + 2 (filter-honesty) = 25 of 40. **Probe scaffolding:** transient `supabase/functions/probe-fp048-news/` edge function deployed for investigation only and removed in this same PR (investigation scaffolding, not signal code — per FP-047 Phase-0 precedent). |
| **Architecture branch (recorded from evidence — both bounds presented)** | **Branch B — global feed paged single-invocation.** `/v2/reference/news?limit=1000&order=desc&sort=published_utc&published_utc.gte={as_of-7d}` returns a global time-ordered feed; pagination via `next_url`. **Per-call arithmetic at observed shape:** one page = 1000 items ≈ 6.3 s wall (mostly server-side time, not client-side decode). **Volume estimate:** the global feed yielded 1000 items "right now" with `next_url_present=true` immediately — assuming articles publish at roughly steady rate, a 7-day window contains approximately 7 × `(items_per_day)` items; with current observed publishing density and at `limit=1000` per page, **a 7-day window requires an estimated 35–70 pages** (5k–10k items/day × 7 days ÷ 1000/page). Pre-flight wall: 35–70 × ~3-6 s = ~3-7 minutes worst case; the orchestrator must run as chunked-coordinator/worker per the FP-043 template OR be split across multiple invocations on the FP-045 queue-engine. Phase 1 picks single-invocation vs chunked based on measured throughput against the 150 s HTTP wall. **Both-bounds discipline (Catalog #39):** *latency-bound* — 70 pages × ~3 s sequential = 210 s > 150 s wall → chunking required worst-case; *rate-bound* — Polygon rate cap on the news endpoint is **operator-supplied dashboard number (not citable from plan docs; same gap as FMP 750/min in FP-047)** — the cron handler's `TokenBucket` will enforce whatever ceiling operator provides at Phase 3 arm-up. Both bounds are recorded; Phase 1 implementation may not assume either is non-binding without re-measuring against the cap. **Branch A — per-symbol** (`?ticker={t}&published_utc.gte={d-7}&limit=1000`): 839 calls/run × ~0.4 s = ~5.5 minutes single-invocation; same chunking requirement; **strictly more expensive than Branch B** because Branch A duplicates multi-ticker articles across each tagged symbol's call. **Branch B is the architecture-of-record** for this FP; Branch A retained only as the not-chosen branch with the arithmetic preserved. |

NOT silently dropped (axiom 3).</td></tr><tr><td>Universe coverage</td><td>20/20 sampled names had ≥1 article in 7d (median ~7 articles/name)</td><td>§(h) Sparse-coverage names emit `no_articles_in_window` typed skip; Phase-7 evidence will measure unrecovered fraction and whether floor/regime is needed (NOT pre-committed).</td></tr></table> |

| **Architecture row (REVISED 2026-06-10 — supersedes original "Branch A: `/stable/price-target-latest-news` global feed-paged single invocation")** | **Branch A+H ("history-augmented"): Phase 1 fetcher pair.** (1) `/stable/price-target-latest-news?page=N&limit=100` paged ~35-37 calls for event discovery (unchanged from original Branch A). Events with no recovered same-analyst prior emit typed skip `revision_prior_unavailable`, contribute zero to the panel, and are counted in observability (Phase-7 ablation will measure whether the unrecovered-fraction's IC contribution materially changes the signal, gating any future Option-3-style fallback DEC). Pre-flight arithmetic (Phase-3 will re-state with measured fetch counts): event discovery 37 calls + per-symbol history ≤839 calls/day worst-case (one per universe name) at 750/min cap → ≤1.2 min wall = WELL within the 150s × multi-invocation budget per the same chunked-coordinator/worker template if needed; sequential single-invocation likely sufficient at ≤120s, but the Phase-3 build picks single-invocation vs FP-043 chunked-template based on measured throughput. Queue engine (FP-045) still not used. |

### FP-046: ROI-Enhancement Roadmap Bundle (DEC-054) — Squeeze-Guard, Trend-Quality, Quality, Regime, IC-Diagnostic, Asymmetric Sizing, Drawdown-Scaling

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **ID** | FP-046 (next-free after FP-045; grep-verified at HEAD `32c16407` — no prior allocation). |
| **Status** | approved-roadmap (DEC-054, operator 2026-06-10). Documentation-only container FP — authorizes NO build. Each enumerated item below ships via its own operator-authorized build FP at its assigned phase. |
| **Problem** | Operator-initiated strategic review on 2026-06-10 surfaced the momentum-exhaustion / reversal-risk concern (longs already topped, shorts already bottomed) as a structural class question against the 9-signal stack. The review (operator concern → supervisor analysis grounded in Da/Gurun/Warachka FIP RFS 2014, Novy-Marx 2013, Daniel-Moskowitz 2016, Barroso-Santa Clara 2015, George-Hwang JF 2004, EFMA 2025 crowded-short evidence, S3 short-squeeze retracement profile → second-opinion review → supervisor reconciliation) concluded: the spec already neutralizes the concern substantially by construction (12-1 skip-month in #6; Signal #7 reversal as CRITICAL anti-exhaustion; 5 of 9 signals information-flow rather than price-trend; learned combiner interactions; §3.3e SI>25% short exclusion). FOUR evidence-backed gaps remain (squeeze-guard, trend-quality, quality, regime). THREE additional items (long-vs-short IC diagnostic, asymmetric sizing, drawdown-conditional gross-scaling) are recorded so downstream consumers are designed for them. |
| **Resolution shape (R-table — roadmap only, each item is its own future build FP)** | **R2 — Squeeze-guard short-book veto (P0, Phase 2.10, build FP required).** Extend §3.3e: short-book entry vetoed when `(SI > 20% of float) AND (days-to-cover > 5) AND (5-day return > 0)`. DTC = SI ÷ 20d ADV, computed from existing Signal #5 + bars data; additionally exposed as a combiner feature. Rationale: squeeze retracement profile (S3: 50-70% retracement within 30-60 days) makes mid-squeeze shorts the tail-loss case; crowdedness research (EFMA 2025) finds crowding subsumes raw SI. $0 vendor cost. Highest-priority because portfolio-survival, not marginal ROI. **R5 — Long-vs-short IC diagnostic (P0, Phase 7 ablation spec, spec-only).** Phase 7 ablation MUST produce a per-signal × per-side IC table (long-tail IC vs short-tail IC, all 9 signals). Motivation: structural long-bias suspicion in #4 (insider purchases informative; sales noise), #1 (downgrade stickiness), #8 (negative-news overreaction). Gates R6. **R1 — Trend-quality combiner features (P1, Phase 2.11, build FP required).** Two new combiner feature pairs computed from the SAME Polygon daily bars Signal #6 already uses: (a) information discreteness `ID = sign(formation return) × (%negative days − %positive days)` over the T-21 → T-252 formation window (Da/Gurun/Warachka FIP, RFS 2014: momentum +5.94% → −2.07% over 6mo from continuous → discrete info at equal cumulative return); (b) formation-period realized volatility (GRJMOM lineage). Signal #6 itself is NOT modified; the combiner learns the interaction. **Recorded risk:** FIP's documented horizon is 6 months vs the §6.2 10-trading-day labels; the effect may not survive the horizon mismatch — Phase 7 ablation is the arbiter and retirement is a fully acceptable outcome. $0 vendor cost. **R3 — Signal #10: quality / gross-profitability (P1, Phase 2.12, build FP required).** GP/A (Novy-Marx 2013) from FMP Premium fundamentals endpoints (already subscribed — $0 marginal). Slow-moving, annually-refreshed inputs; within-sector z; non-critical class; standard (value, is_present) combiner pair. Signal count moves to 10 — within the spec's ~12 pre-ablation ceiling (signal-count discipline preserved verbatim). **R4 — Market-state regime features (P2, folded into the Phase 3 combiner build spec).** 2-3 features: trailing market return (sign + magnitude), trailing market realized vol — letting the ranker learn momentum-crash-state downweighting (Daniel-Moskowitz lineage). Spec-only now; lands with the combiner FP. **R6 — Asymmetric book sizing (P2, Phase 4, CONDITIONAL on R5 evidence).** If R5 shows materially weaker short-side IC, re-derive long/short counts (currently 20/20) from realized signal quality. No pre-commitment to any number; the diagnostic decides. **R7 — Drawdown-conditional gross-exposure scaling (P2, Phase 4/5 build; DEC clause now so downstream consumers of combiner output are designed for it).** Momentum-crash mitigation per Barroso–Santa Clara lineage. Specific rule parameters EXPLICITLY NOT set here — calibrated at Phase 7, not hardcoded. The clause reserves the architectural slot: portfolio construction consumes a gross-scaling multiplier input (default 1.0 until the rule ships). |

### FP-045: Generalized Cursor-Drain Queue-Worker (DEC-047) for Rate-Capped Signals — PEAD Re-Shell + Signal #3 Revival

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **ID** | FP-045 (next-free after FP-044; grep-verified at HEAD — only prior mention was FP-044's "Out of Scope" forward-reference, no allocation collision). |
| **Status** | Phase 0 (governance intake — this entry + INC-72 + `ai-failure-modes.md` entries) drafted; Phases 1–4 staged per the operator-authorized phased prompt with a supervisor STOP between phases. |
| **Problem** | INC-72 surfaced the second instance of the rate-bound single-invocation synchronous fan-out anti-pattern: FP-044's `longshort-pead-compute` 504'd at the 150s HTTP wall on its first operator test-fire and its isolate was terminated at ~190s with **zero `signal_compute_log` rows, zero `signal_observations` rows, and zero application log lines** (execution `11a267bc-ee77-4d54-a0ac-1571749f98cb`, 2026-06-09 20:21 UTC). The PEAD orchestrator ships with a vendor-cap arithmetic defect in its header (`pLimitedMap` concurrency-5 claimed "≤10 req/sec... well under the cap" — 10 req/sec = 600/min vs Finnhub's 300/min ceiling; concurrency-based throttling is latency-dependent and unbounded). At the correctly paced 5 rps cap, 1,678 Finnhub calls take 335s (~395s at the operator-measured 4.25 rps effective) — exceeds the 150s HTTP wall outright, approaches/matches the 400s Pro background-worker budget. Same class as FP-043's pre-token-bucket Tradier path (caught pre-production); promoted now to a structural class by this second firing. The DEC-047 cursor-drain queue-worker is the operator-approved resolution shape; this FP implements it once, generalized for both known rate-capped consumers (PEAD on Finnhub 300/min + options-flow on Tradier 120/min — the latter un-parks Signal #3 per FP-043's park clause and closes DW-095). |
| **Resolution shape (per-phase)** | **Phase 0 (this PR scope):** INC-72 record; `ai-failure-modes.md` §12.10 entries (supervisor-side: Phase-2 fresh-clone verification checked purity/enums/guards but not vendor-cap arithmetic, detection one step late at empirical 504; executor-side: rate-comment asserting 600/min "well under" a 300/min cap, gates structurally cannot catch vendor-cap math); FP-045 entry; FP-044 Status forward-fix to `complete-pending-reshell`. Docs only; gate 4; commit; STOP. **Phase 1 — schema:** MIG-082 introduces `signal_queue_runs`/`signal_queue_cursor`/`signal_queue_staging`/`signal_queue_skips` (generalized per `signal_id` — one table set, NOT per-signal tables); RLS per established deny-write/service-role pattern; idempotent; staging TTL handled by the orphan-sweeper rather than a separate cron; §22.5.1 live-DB read. STOP. **Phase 2 — generic engine:** `_shared/longshort-signals/shared/queue-worker/{queue-config,slice-worker,finalizer,orphan-sweeper}.ts` + edge handlers `longshort-queue-init` / `longshort-queue-slice` (serialized across signals so vendor caps never stack) / `longshort-queue-sweeper`. REUSES `token-bucket.ts` (productionClock-routed), `pLimitedMap`, `signal-types.ts`, `persist-signal-compute-log.ts`. No cadence constants (DEC-048). Per-slice audit event = INC-72 instrumentation fix. Pre-flight arithmetic row (calls/slice × rate vs 150s wall) recorded in PR evidence as the new executor discipline. Gates 2+4+11. STOP. **Phase 3 — PEAD consumer (re-shell):** queue-config registration consuming FP-044's `FinnhubEpsEstimateFetcher` + `FinnhubEarningsFetcher` + `computePead` UNTOUCHED. Synchronous-orchestrator path retired from cron entry; FP-044 orchestrator rate-comment defect corrected in same commit (file retained for the per-ticker arm). MIG-083 job_registry rows (DISARMED; DEC-048 interim language; slot non-overlapping). `JOB_ID_TO_SIGNAL_ID` entry + drift sentinels. `function-index.md` + `event-index.md` + `pead.md` addendum + queue-worker module doc (DEC-047 lineage) SAME-PR. §22.5.1. Gates + pre-flight arithmetic. STOP — then operator test-fire of the PEAD queue path validates before Phase 4. **Phase 4 — Signal #3 revival:** queue-config registration consuming FP-043's `TradierOptionsChainFetcher` + `computeOptionsFlow` + per-ticker arm UNTOUCHED. `signal_registry` `options_flow` planned→live (truth-in-telemetry precedent); DW-095 closed (evidence: this FP); DEC-047 status → implemented; FP-043 closure addendum. MIG-084 + §22.5.1. Operator test-fire validates. Closes the rate-capped class. |
| **Scope (Phase 0 — this PR)** | Doc edits only: this FP-045 entry; INC-72 (incidental-findings.md); two §12.10 operational-log entries in `ai-failure-modes.md` (supervisor-side blind-spot + executor-side rate-math defect); FP-044 Status field forward-fix per §22.3(e) to `complete-pending-reshell (orchestration shell superseded by FP-045; compute/fetchers/handlers reused)`. No code; no migration; no reference-index registrations (those land in Phase 2/3 same-PR as the engine + consumer registrations per Rule 6). |
| **Out of Scope** | NO changes to `compute-pead.ts`, `compute-options-flow.ts`, any fetcher logic, `z-score-normalize`, `persist-signal-compute-log` row shape, or `SignalSkipReason` enum (the queue engine maps existing skips, does not invent new ones). NO cron ENABLE on the queue handlers (operator test-fire-then-restore per DEC-040 / DEC-043). NO Signal #1 (`analyst-revisions`) or Signal #8 (`news`) work — those are separate FPs against the same engine once landed. NO `waitUntil` reliance — the queue-worker's design intent is that every invocation completes well inside the 150s wall, removing background-execution variance from the attestation path. Root cause is a vendor-cap × call-count × wall-budget architectural mismatch (`pead-orchestrator.ts` ships with header rate-math claiming 600/min is "well under" Finnhub's 300/min cap), not a code-correctness defect in the compute or fetcher layers. | **Problem** | Phase 2.6 deliverable: Signal #2 (post-earnings announcement drift, 20-day decay) per CROSSWIND §4.4.6. The signal requires a per-quarter analyst-consensus row carrying `epsAvg`, `epsHigh`, `epsLow`, `numberAnalysts` for the JUST-REPORTED quarter — needed to compute SUE = `(actual − consensus) / σ_proxy` with the DEC-051 range-proxy denominator and the DEC-052 N≥2 floor. The pre-build vendor audit (ACT-160 / chat-labeled "ACT-164") confirmed FMP `/stable/analyst-estimates?period=quarter` returns ONLY future-quarter rows — every already-reported quarter is gone from the endpoint by the time SUE is computed, so DEC-051 and DEC-052 cannot be satisfied on FMP alone. The earlier DEC-049 single-vendor framing is therefore superseded by DEC-053 (split-vendor: FMP for Signal #1, Finnhub for Signal #2). |
| **Resolution (Phase 1 ONLY in this PR)** | (a) NEW `FinnhubEpsEstimateFetcher` (`_shared/longshort-signals/shared/finnhub-eps-estimate-fetcher.ts`) wrapping `https://finnhub.io/api/v1/stock/eps-estimate?freq=quarterly`. Entitlement-aware (401/403 → `subscription_gated`, 404/empty → `data_unavailable`). Anti-phantom: drops any row missing `epsAvg` / `epsHigh` / `epsLow` / `numberAnalysts`; drops `numberAnalysts<=0` (vendor regression vs zero-coverage 404 distinction). Returns `{period, epsAvg, epsHigh, epsLow, numberAnalysts}` ASC-sorted; consumer filters as_of-relative downstream (no client-side filter in the fetcher — preserves replay-safety). Embedded `verifyFilterHonored` + `verifyFieldsPresent` static helpers. (b) NEW `FinnhubEarningsFetcher` (`_shared/longshort-signals/shared/finnhub-earnings-fetcher.ts`) wrapping `https://finnhub.io/api/v1/stock/earnings` — BARE ARRAY response with defensive type-guard (not a silent cast — a future wrapped-shape vendor regression would otherwise silently empty results). Returns `{period, actual, estimate}` with `actual` typed-null for future quarters. Same status mapping + dual-axis helpers. (c) Pacing decision (recorded in fetcher header): Finnhub 300/min ≫ 839 names; `pLimitedMap` bounded-concurrency only. NO token-bucket — deliberate contrast with FP-043's Tradier path where 120/min was the binding constraint. (d) Dual-axis verify + LOOK-AHEAD GATE re-run live with the actual fetcher classes — operational-window verdict 0/13 drifted → CLEAN; deep-history >4yr drift (13/392) recorded as observability finding, OUTSIDE the §4.4.6 stale-earnings cutoff so it cannot reach production SUE compute. |
| **Resolution (Phase 2 — staged; NOT in this PR)** | NEW pure `compute-pead.ts` (SUE × `exp(−trading_days_since_earnings / 20)`; typed skips `stale_earnings` / `insufficient_analysts` / `zero_dispersion` / `no_earnings_data` / `fetch_error`; reuses `shared/trading-days.ts` for the trading-day calendar). ### FP-043: Signal #3 — Options Flow Imbalance (5-day, smart-money filtered)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **ID** | FP-043 (next-free after FP-042; grep-verified at HEAD). |
| **Status** | implemented (Phase 1 Tradier fetcher with dual-axis self-checks; Phase 2 compute + orchestrator; Phase 3 chunked coordinator/worker + token-bucket pacer + cron + manual handlers; Phase 4 MIG-078 disarmed `job_registry` seed + `signal_registry` planned→live flip + full doc set). Cron wiring + enable-flip pending separate operator-run DEC-043 attestation. |
| **Problem** | Phase 2.7 deliverable: Signal #3 (options flow imbalance, 5-day, smart-money filtered) per CROSSWIND §4.4.7 is the highest-cost signal in the stack ("~30-50% of daily compute budget"). It introduces a new vendor (Tradier production), the first chunked coordinator/worker architecture under a strict 120 req/min rate cap, and the first signal subject to a dual-axis fetcher discipline (filter-honored + fields-present). |
| **Resolution** | (a) NEW production Tradier fetcher (`tradier-options-chain-fetcher.ts`) wired against `api.tradier.com/v1`, entitlement-aware (401/403 → `subscription_gated`, 404/empty → `data_unavailable`), with embedded `verifyFilterHonored()` + `verifyFieldsPresent()` self-checks (the latter is INC-71's lesson). Tradier-normalisation helper handles the single-result-as-bare-object quirk. (b) NEW pure compute (`compute-options-flow.ts`) — 4-case direction classifier (call-buy-at-ask=+1, put-buy-at-ask=−1, call-sell-at-bid=−1, put-sell-at-bid=+1; sign error inverts the signal — all 4 cases + boundary equalities pinned by tests); smart-money filter (`volume>=100`, `DTE>=7`, `|delta|<=0.65`); 48-hour-half-life exponential decay keyed off `as_of` (no wall-clock); `MIN_QUALIFYING_PRINTS=5` floor; div-by-zero guard. (c) NEW orchestrator + chunk-runner + coordinator: shards the universe into N=6 strides, fans out HTTPS to N parallel workers, each worker paced by a shared token bucket at ~0.28 req/sec (≈ 100 req/min total = 6 × 0.28; ~15% headroom under the 120/min cap; absorbs 429 retries via the fetcher's [1s,2s,4s] backoff). Partial-failure honesty: a failed worker emits `fetch_error` skips for every ticker in its shard rather than silently dropping data (the Signal #4 lesson). (d) NEW `longshort-options-flow-worker` (CRON_SECRET, returns per-shard signals+skips); `longshort-options-flow-compute` (cron coordinator); `longshort-options-flow-compute-manual` (POST + JWT + `longshort.manage`, with `as_of` parsing + future-date guard). (e) Extended `SignalSkipReason` with `no_qualifying_flow` + same-commit `aggregateSkipCounts` seeding + `persist-signal-compute-log_test.ts` exact-match assertion update (FP-041 stale-test lesson applied). (f) Extended `JOB_ID_TO_SIGNAL_ID` + drift sentinel (cross-references `options-flow-orchestrator.ts::SIGNAL_ID`). (g) MIG-078 disarmed `job_registry` seed (schedule `'0 22 * * 1-5'`) + `signal_registry` planned→live flip (status='live', job_registry_id, stale_after_hours=72). signal_id locked: `'options_flow_imbalance_5d'`. |
| **Scope** | NEW `_shared/longshort-signals/shared/tradier-options-chain-fetcher.ts` (+ `_test.ts` 27 tests). NEW `_shared/longshort-signals/options-flow/{compute-options-flow,options-flow-orchestrator,options-flow-chunk-runner,options-flow-coordinator,token-bucket}.ts` (+ tests; 40+ total). NEW `supabase/functions/longshort-options-flow-{worker,compute,compute-manual}/{index.ts,index_test.ts}`. NEW MIG-078 (`job_registry` INSERT disarmed + `signal_registry` UPDATE planned→live). | **Resolution** | Mirror the proven Signal #5 architecture (FP-041) with the §4.4.4-driven additions: (a) NEW entitlement-aware `PolygonForm4Fetcher` — HTTP 403 → typed `subscription_gated`, 404 → `data_unavailable`, neither throws / neither fabricates; returns BOTH `record_type='transaction'` and `record_type='holding'` rows (compute filters). (b) NEW pure compute module `compute-insider.ts` exporting `filterQualifyingTransactions` (P all; S only where `aff_10b5_one===false`; M/C/A/G excluded; first gate drops holding-only rows), `classifyRoleWeight` (3-tier title-heuristic per DEC-044 — C-suite/President 1.0, NEO-proxy via "Chief X Officer"/EVP/SVP 0.7, 10%+ owner 0.5, generic Section-16 officer 0.4, independent director 0.3; highest-applicable-weight tie-break; `(?<!vice\s)\bpresident\b` guard prevents "Executive Vice President" leaking into tier 1), and `computeInsiderSignal` (Σ shares × price × sign × role_weight × exp(-age/14) / market_cap; `null` on zero qualifying transactions OR non-positive market_cap; replay-deterministic via injected `as_of`). (c) NEW `createInsiderOrchestrator` with THREE parallel side-input fetchers per ticker (form-4 + shares-outstanding + price for market_cap), translating each entitlement-aware unavailability into the matching `SignalSkipReason`; surfaces a new `no_qualifying_transactions` skip when the filter empties the row set (the EXPECTED case for most names). (d) Cron handler `longshort-insider-compute` (verifyCronSecret + productionClock + 3-fetcher context + 3 audit events). (e) Manual sibling `longshort-insider-compute-manual` (POST + JWT + `longshort.manage` + dual audit envelope). (f) MIG-077 disarmed `job_registry` seed with daily-after-close schedule `'0 19 * * 1-5'` + `signal_registry` planned→live flip (status='live', job_registry_id, cadence note, stale_after_hours=48). (g) Extended `SignalSkipReason` with `no_qualifying_transactions` + same-commit `aggregateSkipCounts` seeding + `persist-signal-compute-log_test.ts` exact-match assertion update (FP-041 stale-test lesson applied). (h) Extended `JOB_ID_TO_SIGNAL_ID` + drift sentinel. signal_id locked: `'insider_transactions_90d'`. |

**Pre-build gate (operator-confirmed)** — Live probe of `/stocks/filings/vX/form-4` against the production POLYGON key returned 200 + the documented field set (`transaction_code`, `aff_10b5_one`, `transaction_shares`, `transaction_price_per_share`, `transaction_date`, `transaction_acquired_disposed`, `is_director`, `is_officer`, `is_ten_percent_owner`, `not_subject_to_section_16`, `officer_title`, `record_type`, `security_type`). **Closure** — Landed at execution commit. Signal #4 is the fourth of nine live (in the registry sense — code/migration shipped, cron disarmed). 4 live + 5 planned + 1 planned composite. Validation path: operator POST `longshort-insider-compute-manual` with `{ "as_of": "YYYY-MM-DD" }` → expected SPARSE result (most names `no_qualifying_transactions` → `is_present=0` is NORMAL, not failure; a minority carries real weighted-decay z-scores). Cron wiring per DEC-043 is a separate operator step. Authority: ACT-154.

**Addendum (ACT-155, 2026-06-09) — Market-wide Form 4 fetch (CPU-limit fix; Constitution Rule 8 supersession via addendum).** First post-deploy fire died with HTTP 546 `WORKER_RESOURCE_LIMIT` (~2 s edge-isolate CPU budget exceeded ~6 s after boot — NOT memory, NOT wall-time). Root cause: per-ticker fetch across ~839 universe tickers = ~2517 HTTPS round-trips (Form 4 + shares + price ×839) + 839 JSON parses + per-row regex/decay loops — ~3–5× short-interest's CPU footprint. Fix: replace per-ticker Form 4 calls with ONE market-wide paginated call to `/stocks/filings/vX/form-4?transaction_date.gte=…&lte=…` (NO `ticker=`), follow `next_url` cursor (≤50 pages × 1000 rows), group locally by `tickers[0]` (primary-issuer attribution). Live-probe 2026-06-09 confirmed entitlement on the no-ticker variant. Orchestrator restructured to run the existing `filterQualifyingTransactions` per-universe-ticker BEFORE any per-ticker market-cap fetch — tickers with 0 qualifying rows skip `no_qualifying_transactions` without any shares/price HTTPS call. Compute / filter / classifier / z-score / persist BYTE-UNCHANGED. New `PolygonForm4Fetcher.fetchForm4MarketWide` + `Form4Row.tickers?: string[]` + `Form4MarketWideResult` type. The per-ticker `fetchForm4` is retained for the manual single-ticker debug path. +6 fetcher tests (single-page grouping, next_url pagination, 403/404 typed-missing, missing-tickers drop, multi-ticker tickers[0] attribution); 12 orchestrator tests preserved BYTE-IDENTICAL through the mock-shape change. ESLint 0 errors on the 4 changed files; no `any`; no `eslint-disable`; no `Date.now()`; div-by-zero guard preserved. Authority: ACT-155. Pattern becomes the template for the remaining feed-signals (#1 / #2 / #3 / #8) — any per-ticker-across-the-universe fetch would hit the same CPU wall.

**Second addendum (ACT-156, 2026-06-09) — Signal #4 disarmed; Polygon Form 4 endpoint silently ignores filters (INC-70); P2 double-tally fix; EDGAR rebuild deferred (DW-094). Constitution Rule 8 supersession via addendum; original entry + ACT-155 addendum preserved verbatim.**

After the ACT-155 market-wide rewrite, the next fire returned HTTP 200 but with the impossible telemetry `fetch_error: 839` AND `no_qualifying_transactions: 839` simultaneously (1678 skips for 839-ticker universe; `signal_compute_log.run_id='1021808b-4b1c-4b04-bb45-d989d56b5193'`). Investigation surfaced TWO distinct problems:

**P1 (INC-70 — data correctness, severity HIGH).** A 9-variant probe of `/stocks/filings/vX/form-4` against the deployed `POLYGON_API_KEY` returned **byte-identical 1.85 MB / 1000-row firehose responses** for: single-ticker, `ticker.any_of`, comma-list, `ticker.in`, pipe-delimited, repeated-param, market-wide 90-day, AND market-wide 14-day. `ticker=AAPL` returned rows NOT containing AAPL (top buckets: DELL=312, FCNCA=41, SSMR=38). Only `limit=` was honored. The endpoint silently ignores `ticker` (every syntax) AND `transaction_date.*` — Candidates A (multi-ticker), B (date-chunked), C (per-ticker) are ALL impossible at this entitlement tier. The original FP-042 per-ticker fetcher was therefore broken since deploy: it fetched the same firehose 839×, locally filtered by `tickers[0]===ticker` (would yield ~0 rows per name since our universe isn't dominant in the firehose), and the compute layer marked each ticker `no_qualifying_transactions` — perfectly indistinguishable from the EXPECTED sparse profile. **Phantom signal: looked sparse, was actually wrong.** The ACT-155 CPU failure was the lucky trigger that exposed the silent data bug.

**P2 (telemetry double-tally).** The ACT-155 catch block pushed 839 per-ticker `fetch_error` skips on a market-wide throw, then assigned `marketWide = { kind:'rows', rowsByTicker: new Map() }` WITHOUT returning, falling through into the qualifying-filter loop which pushed 839 MORE `no_qualifying_transactions` skips. Two skip classes per ticker, 1678 total — telemetrically impossible.

**This addendum's scope.** (a) **`insider-orchestrator.ts`** — the market-wide-throw catch block now returns `outcome='failed'` with `failure_reason='form4 market-wide fetch failed: <thrown message>'` and `skipped: []`, short-circuiting before the qualifying-filter loop. A run can never again show two skip classes for the same ticker. The `kind:'unavailable'` branch (typed entitlement state — 403/404 — neither a throw nor a fetcher error) is UNCHANGED. (b) **`signal_registry`** — `insider_transactions_90d.status` flipped `'live' → 'planned'` via data UPDATE (no new MIG); `job_registry.longshort.insider.compute.enabled` stays `false`. The non-critical signal rule (§6.5) means the combiner imputes Signal #4's absence and the long-short stack continues to rank on momentum / reversal / short-interest. (c) **FP-042 compute / classifier / filter / z-score code is UNTOUCHED** — it is correct and reused as-is when the EDGAR fetcher lands (DW-094). Only the data-acquisition layer needs replacing. (d) **Docs** — INC-70, DW-094, this addendum, ACT-156, `insider-transactions.md` ACT-156 addendum, NEW `_pattern-vendor-fetcher-filter-honesty.md`.

**Pattern lesson (codified, applies to remaining feed-signals).** Every vendor-endpoint fetcher MUST implement a `verifyFilterHonored()` pre-flight: probe with a filter value that SHOULD return zero rows (e.g. `ticker=ZZZZZZZZ`, far-future date) and assert the response is empty — NOT a firehose. If a should-be-empty query returns data, the filter is silently ignored and client-side filtering will produce wrong data. This would have caught INC-70 on day one. Bound as a §22.3-style pre-flight item for Signals #1 (analyst), #2 (PEAD), #3 (options), #8 (news) — each has its own vendor endpoint whose filters must be probed before trust. Documented at `docs/04-modules/longshort/signals/_pattern-vendor-fetcher-filter-honesty.md`.

**Evidence.** Gate 4 ESLint on changed files — 0 errors, 0 warnings. Gate 4 Vitest — `50 files / 388 tests passed`. Gate 11 Deno scoped to FP-042 surfaces — `70 passed / 0 failed`. Live-DB §22.5.1 confirms `signal_registry.status='planned'` and `job_registry.enabled=false`. **Original FP-042 entry + ACT-155 addendum preserved verbatim per Constitution Rule 8.** Forward-pointer: INC-70 + DW-094 + ACT-156.

#### FP-041 Addendum (revision-fix) — `si_pct_float` derivation (Option A)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Post-execution manual validation (operator) returned `outcome=completed`, `universe_size=839`, `persisted_count=0`, `insufficient_history=839`, all other skip counts = 0 — entitlement + network + history were fine, but every ticker normalized to zero reports. Read-only investigation traced the cause to `polygon-short-interest-fetcher.ts` `normalizeRow` requiring a `short_percent_of_float` field that Polygon's `/stocks/v1/short-interest` endpoint **does not return**; sample-response field set (verified against live docs) is `ticker / settlement_date / short_interest / avg_daily_volume / days_to_cover`. The anti-phantom guard correctly refused to fabricate a percentage and the failure surfaced cleanly as `insufficient_history` instead of producing garbage z-scores. |
| **Decision** | Option A — derive `si_pct_float = short_interest / share_class_shares_outstanding`, preserving the spec §4.4.3 %-of-float denomination. Option B (`days_to_cover` substitution) was REJECTED — it deviates from §4.4.3 and is inconsistent with §3.3e (also % -of-float) and would require a fresh DEC. The shares-outstanding side input is already available from the SAME Polygon reference endpoint that universe-enrichment uses (`/v3/reference/tickers/{ticker}` returns `share_class_shares_outstanding`); NO new subscription entitlement is introduced. |
| **Conscious approximation (documented per §2 axiom 4)** | The reference endpoint provides ONLY CURRENT shares-outstanding; the signal compares SI at two HISTORICAL report dates (T and T-2 ≈ 30 calendar days). We denominate BOTH historical SI counts with current shares-outstanding. Defensible because shares-outstanding is slow-moving (corporate actions on the order of quarters/years) relative to SI swings (twice-monthly, often orders-of-magnitude); the percentage change is dominated by the SI numerator change. Point-in-time shares-outstanding history (which would account for splits / buybacks shifting the denominator) is not exposed by this endpoint and would require a FINRA + EDGAR cross-source (same backup roadmap as the SI fetcher; intentionally deferred). The approximation is pinned in three places by design: orchestrator file-level comment, `function-index.md` orchestrator row, and `docs/04-modules/longshort/signals/short-interest-change.md`. |
| **Scope** | NEW `_shared/longshort-signals/shared/polygon-shares-outstanding-fetcher.ts` (+ `_test.ts`; 10 tests including missing/zero/negative/NaN → typed-absence; entitlement-aware). EDIT `polygon-short-interest-fetcher.ts` — return shape now `{ report_date, short_interest }` (raw share count); normalizeRow requires finite `short_interest >= 0` and drops missing/negative; zero is VALID (genuinely no shorts). EDIT `short-interest-orchestrator.ts` — context now includes `sharesOutstanding: PolygonSharesOutstandingFetcher`; parallel `Promise.all` fetch per ticker; map raw reports to `ShortInterestReport { si_pct_float }` via derivation; new `missing_shares_outstanding` skip when denominator unavailable; defensive zero-shares re-check inside orchestrator (orchestrator catches divide-by-zero even if fetcher's guard regresses). EDIT `signal-types.ts` — add `missing_shares_outstanding` to `SignalSkipReason` (now 7 members). EDIT `_shared/persist-signal-compute-log.ts` — seed `missing_shares_outstanding: 0` in `aggregateSkipCounts`. EDIT `persist-signal-compute-log_test.ts` — 3 exact-match-assertion updates (stale-test lesson applied). EDIT both handlers (`longshort-short-interest-compute{,-manual}/index.ts`) — construct `PolygonSharesOutstandingFetcher` and pass into ctx (KEEP-IN-SYNC pair). EDIT handler source-sentinel tests — assert `sharesOutstanding` field present. Docs same-PR: this addendum; `function-index.md` (NEW shares-outstanding fetcher entry; UPDATE short-interest fetcher entry + orchestrator entry with derivation + conscious-approximation note + 7-member enum); `docs/04-modules/longshort/signals/short-interest-change.md` (denominator-derivation paragraph + new dataflow diagram + new skip-reason row + dedicated "Conscious approximation" section); ACT-153a (this revision-fix). NO new permissions / routes / migrations / npm dependencies / env-vars. |
| **Anti-pattern guards (Tier A)** | (1) NO fabricated denominator — missing shares → typed `missing_shares_outstanding` skip; (2) DIVIDE-BY-ZERO guard at TWO layers — fetcher rejects zero/negative/NaN/Infinity shares (8 dedicated tests), orchestrator defensively re-checks (test (17) lying-fetcher mock proves it); (3) the spec formula `-1 × (SI%[T] − SI%[T-2])` is preserved — only the SOURCE of `si_pct_float` changes from "phantom field read" to "raw count divided by shares"; (4) NO wall-clock drift — orchestrator timestamps still derive from `as_of`; (5) the conscious-approximation comment is mandatory (the approximation must be VISIBLE — pinned in 3 places). |
| **Verification** | Gate 11 (Deno) scoped to FP-041 + affected shared files: **77 passed / 0 failed** (`polygon-short-interest-fetcher_test.ts` 10/10, `polygon-shares-outstanding-fetcher_test.ts` 10/10, `compute-short-interest_test.ts` 13/13, `short-interest-orchestrator_test.ts` 17/17 — incl. 5 new derivation-path tests, `persist-signal-compute-log_test.ts` 9/9, `longshort-short-interest-compute/index_test.ts` 9/9, `longshort-short-interest-compute-manual/index_test.ts` 9/9). Full Gate 11 (excluding 3 pre-existing broken universe test files unrelated to FP-041): **568 passed / 11 failed** (all 11 failures are pre-existing profile/user-management integration tests requiring a live test Supabase, unrelated to FP-041 — same failures on main). Gate 4 Vitest: **388 passed / 50 files**. Gate 4 ESLint scoped to changed FP-041 files: **0 warnings / 0 errors** (the 15 pre-existing warnings are all in unrelated files on main). No `any`. |

### FP-045 Phase 4 Pacing Revision-Fix Addendum (Rule 8 — Forward-Pointer, Original Preserved)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Addendum Date** | 2026-06-10 |
| **Authority** | Operator REVISION-FIX mode ("Pacing reconciliation (the blocker)") following the Phase 4 closure addendum above. |
| **Defect** | The Phase 4 closure pinned `callsPerName=1` in `options-flow-queue-registration.ts` while the `TradierOptionsChainFetcher` per-ticker arm makes TWO wire calls (`/markets/options/expirations` + `/markets/options/chains`). The closure addendum's arithmetic row computed `(80 × 2) / 1.7 ≈ 94.1s` — citing the TRUE wire-call count — but the registration carried `callsPerName=1` and the adapter wrapped HTTP in a SECOND `pacedHttpFetch`-bound `TokenBucket(ratePerSec=1.7)`. Two buckets at the same rate, serialized: per-ticker time = worker-bucket(1/1.7=0.59s) + adapter-bucket(2/1.7=1.18s) ≈ 1.76s; 80-slice ≈ 141s, dangerously close to the 150s HTTP wall and inconsistent with the documented ≈94.1s budget. The drift sentinel test masked the defect by hand-entering `(sliceSize * 2)` instead of deriving from `callsPerName`. |
| **Trace evidence** | (1) `TradierOptionsChainFetcher.fetchChainForTicker` — one `/markets/options/expirations` call + one `/markets/options/chains` call per ticker = 2 wire calls (file: `_shared/longshort-signals/shared/tradier-options-chain-fetcher.ts`). (2) Pre-fix `options-flow-queue-registration.ts:88-91` constructed `new TokenBucket({ratePerSec:1.7})` + `pacedHttpFetch(bucket, fetch)` and passed `paced` to `TradierOptionsChainFetcher`. (3) `queue-slice-worker.ts:122-133` constructs its own `TokenBucket(ratePerSec=cfg.ratePerSec)` via `defaultBucketFactory` and acquires `cfg.callsPerName` tokens per ticker BEFORE invoking `fetchAndCompute`. (4) Two independent buckets → double-acquisition; TRUE tokens against Tradier wire = 2/ticker via adapter bucket; TRUE effective Tradier wire rate ≈ 1.7 rps (102/min < 120/min cap — never breached) but TRUE per-ticker wall time ≈ 1.76s, not the documented 1.18s. |
| **Fix** | Pacing collapsed to ONE place — the slice-worker bucket (engine-owned). (1) `options-flow-queue-registration.ts` — removed `TokenBucket` + `pacedHttpFetch` import; adapter receives raw `fetch`; `callsPerName` flipped `1 → 2` to match the fetcher's true wire-call count. (2) `options-flow-queue-registration_test.ts` — introduced `TRADIER_WIRE_CALLS_PER_TICKER = 2` constant; arithmetic-budget test now asserts `callsPerName === TRADIER_WIRE_CALLS_PER_TICKER` AND derives `perSliceWire = (sliceSize × callsPerName) / ratePerSec` (no more hand-entered `* 2`); 94.1s pin retained. (3) `docs/04-modules/longshort/signals/queue-worker.md` — arithmetic table row + Phase-4 consumer table updated to `callsPerName=2`; Pacing contract section adds the single-bucket invariant ("adapters MUST pass raw `fetch`"). (4) DW-095 closure cell + this addendum carry the corrected text. |
| **Burst-vs-smooth verification** | With `callsPerName=2`, worker acquires both tokens UPFRONT (`for (let i=0; i<callsPerName; i++) await bucket.acquire()`) then invokes the adapter which makes two HTTP calls back-to-back. Wire-pattern: 2 calls every 1.18s = 1.7 rps average. Tradier's 120/min cap is a minute-window cap (not strict per-second), so the burst-of-2 shape is well-tolerated. The slice cron handler runs synchronously inside `Deno.serve` (`longshort-queue-slice/index.ts`); 94.1s slice + ≤5s finalizer (only on the CAS-winning final slice) ≈ 99s wall, comfortably under the 150s HTTP wall — no `EdgeRuntime.waitUntil` needed, no job-timeout column to raise. |
| **DEC-047 status confirmation** | DEC-047 already carries `Status: implemented (FP-045 generalized engine + Phase 3 PEAD consumer + Phase 4 options-flow consumer; DW-095 closed)` in `docs/08-planning/approved-decisions.md` (grep-verified). No re-flip needed. |
| **MIG-085 §22.5.1 live evidence** | Post-fix live-DB read (2026-06-10): `signal_registry.options_flow_imbalance_5d` → `status='live'`, `cadence='daily (after-close; queue-drained ~11 min; interim per DEC-048 — §4.4.7 5-min intraday target deferred per DEC-046 v2)'`, `planned_phase=NULL`. `job_registry.longshort.options_flow.compute` → `enabled=false`, `status='registered'`, `schedule='0 22 * * 1-5'`, `handler_path='supabase/functions/longshort-options-flow-compute/index.ts'`, description carries the FP-045 Phase 4 queue-shim shape. Both rows preserved per FP-045 §5; MIG-085 metadata-only flip stands. |
| **Gate evidence** | Gate 2 (wall-clock scanner): CLEAN (0 violations on changed files). Gate 4 (CI ESLint command verbatim): `npx eslint .` — 0 errors / 15 baseline warnings. Gate 11 (Deno tests): `options-flow-queue-registration_test.ts` 6 tests green including the new structural assertion `callsPerName === TRADIER_WIRE_CALLS_PER_TICKER`. |
| **Failure-mode escalation** | Promoted to Catalog #39 — "Vendor-cap arithmetic inconsistency between documented budget and code-level config" — per the §12.10 operational-log entry below ("SECOND occurrence" trigger; first firing FP-044 Phase 2 PEAD orchestrator, second firing FP-045 Phase 4 options-flow registration). Codification target: the structural test pattern landed in `options-flow-queue-registration_test.ts` (drift sentinel must derive the arithmetic row from the FETCHER's actual wire-call count, not a hand-entered constant) is the prevention shape; future per-signal registrations MUST follow it. |

### FP-039: Cron-Auth Outage Remediation (atomic env-rotate + redeploy + command-reconcile across 6 consumers)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **ID** | FP-039 (FP-038 reserved for signal-registry spec — NOT consumed here; highest live prior FP at HEAD is FP-037). |
| **Status** | Applied + verified (operator-run out-of-band, 2026-06-08). Bucket-C-unblocking. Supersedes FP-019 as a standalone fix (FP-019 was partial — see FP-019 Addendum below). |
| **Problem** | Every cron consumer (jobids 34/35/36/37/48/51) returned 401 Unauthorized on every fire; `cron.job_run_details.status='succeeded'` concealed the failure. Root cause: three concurrent defects — divergent secret values across cron commands (5 jobs on `ee867b97…`, jobid:48 on `076426…`), deployed `CRON_SECRET` env var matching neither, and INC-63 redeploy-required constraint (env binds at deploy). FP-019's partial corrective aligned 5 command-sides but missed jobid:48 and skipped the env+redeploy step — net effect was 0% cron auth success until FP-039. See INC-69 for full surface story. |
| **Resolution (operator-run, applied + verified 2026-06-08)** | One atomic remediation: (1) generated a new canonical `CRON_SECRET` value; (2) updated the Supabase Edge Function secret `CRON_SECRET` to that value; (3) redeployed all 6 consumers so they bind the new env at runtime (`longshort-momentum-compute`, `longshort-universe-quarterly-refresh`, `job-health-check`, `job-alert-evaluation`, `job-metrics-aggregate`, `job-audit-cleanup`); (4) reconciled all 6 cron commands via `cron.schedule(...)` idempotent upserts so every `X-Cron-Secret` header carries the canonical value. Per §22.5.3 plaintext-secret out-of-band path: no MIG allocated. |
| **End-to-end evidence (DEC-043 standard)** | (a) Platform jobs 34/35 returned **200 `{"state":"succeeded","success":true}`** on schedule 2026-06-08 10:02–11:58 UTC (first 200s in the retention window). (b) Momentum jobid:51 test-fired and produced **two cron-attributable `signal_compute_log` rows** — `completed_at` 2026-06-08 11:52:00 and 11:54:00 UTC, `as_of_date` 2026-06-08, `outcome=completed`, `persisted_count=834` (real wall-clock; not the midnight-manual signature). (c) Byte-match attestation for jobid:51: exactly 1 `cron.job` row, `schedule = '0 20 * * 1-5'` byte-identical to `job_registry.schedule`, `active=true`, command carries resolved project ref + real secret (no placeholder literals). |
| **Scope (atomic, all-or-nothing across 6 consumers)** | Env-var rotate + Edge Function redeploy ×6 + `cron.schedule()` reconcile ×6 in one operator session. Treating this as 6 separate fixes would have left a partial-success state similar to FP-019. |
| **Out of Scope** | Warmup jobs 29-33 (405s — pre-existing, separate INC when addressed). CRON_SECRET rotation to a chat-clean value (deferred to DW-091, which is updated-not-closed — see DW-091 addendum). HEAD c443000 reconciliation (unrelated). Any code/migration/schema/RLS change. |
| **Reference Impact** | `docs/06-tracking/incidental-findings.md` (INC-69); `docs/08-planning/approved-decisions.md` (DEC-043); `docs/00-governance/definition-of-done.md` (new DoD checklist item referencing DEC-043); `docs/06-tracking/action-tracker.md` (ACT-150 + ACT-130); `docs/08-planning/deferred-work-register.md` (DW-091 addendum row); `sql/14_longshort_signal_cron_schedule.sql` (header-comment correction — canonical live-verified pattern is now jobid:51, NOT jobid:48). No new permissions, events, configs, env-vars, migrations, routes, edge functions, shared helpers, or npm deps. No MIG (operator-run plaintext-secret out-of-band per §22.5.3). |
| **Decision ID** | DEC-043 (the new scheduled-job-attestation rule motivated by INC-69 — verbatim codification of "enabled ≠ scheduled ≠ authenticated; attestation requires end-to-end 200 + real artifact row"). |
| **Reviewed By** | Operator |
| **Review Date** | 2026-06-08 |

**Closure** — Applied + verified out-of-band by operator 2026-06-08. The 6-consumer cron-auth path is restored end-to-end; first cron-attributable 200s + artifact rows in the project's retention history landed at 10:02–11:58 + 11:52/11:54 UTC. FP-018 Bucket C now closeable per ACT-130 on stronger evidence than originally planned. Forward-binding: DEC-043 is the binding attestation standard going forward; DW-091 remains open with a hard tripwire to close before Phase 8 (small live capital).

Authority: ACT-150 (FP-039/INC-69 governance authoring) + ACT-130 (FP-018 Bucket C closure).

| **Tier** | **A — financial-critical money path.** This is the FP that places paper orders against the live combiner book. The autonomous-resolution clause + the fallback-as-execution-input clause are load-bearing operator acceptances (see DEC-068 clauses (a) + (b)). |
| **Authority chain** | Operator-ratified DEC-068 (this commit) — investigation (Lovable, charter scoping + DW-138 reframe + §8.9 schema-fit grep) + reconciliation (supervisor, v1 cut + fallback-as-exec-input + spec-anchor verification at §8.6.1 line 109 + §8.6.2 line 187) + **operator amendment** (autonomous three-tier resolution; operator paging reserved for invariant violations only — RATIFICATION of existing spec posture, NOT novel philosophy) + supervisor re-verification (the amendment realigns the charter with spec). The execution layer is the actual time-to-paper-trading constraint — sizing is done, the cron+combiner+book pipeline is live, the only missing piece is the loop that turns target rows into broker calls under bounded, autonomous correctness discipline. |
| **Resolution (v1 cut — minimal coherent paper-order-placement loop)** | **v1.a Delta-computer** (pure compute) — reads target rows + current Alpaca positions, computes `delta = target_qty - current_qty` per ticker; emits a `noop` for held targets matching qty (§1.5 "existing positions are not disturbed" rule). **v1.b §7 pre-flight gate composer** — wraps the existing verify_* surfaces (`verify_halt_status`, `verify_short_availability`, `verify_ssr_status`, `verify_buying_power`, `verify_position`) into a single gate-composer that runs BEFORE every submit attempt; the §7 gates are the PRIMARY defense for pause-class causes (routing correctness is the system's responsibility, not the broker's). **v1.c Sequential submitter** — `POST /v2/orders` one at a time via `AlpacaPaperClient.postJson` (the seam already exists); never two same-symbol same-side opposite-side in flight (ADR-002 wash-trade lock). PAUSE classes (`ssr_violation` / `pdt_block` / persistent-BP) DEFERRED per DEC-068 clause (e). **v1.f `longshort.execute` permission key** — introduced at E5 build concurrent with the first consumer (DEC-032 clause (4); DEC-036 clause (4) retired per DEC-068 clause (d)). **v1.g Triple-evidence closure** — replay fixture + `reconciliation_events` telemetry + Alpaca paper spot-check (E6 DW-138-gated). **Autonomous three-tier resolution per DEC-068 clause (b):** Tier 1 auto-retry per §8.6.2 ladder bounded by `MAX_RETRY_ATTEMPTS=3` + `MAX_SLIPPAGE_BUDGET=50bps`; Tier 2 auto-skip terminal-for-tick with `unfillable_skip` state + re-eligible next tick (book operates one fewer name per §8.6.2 line 187); Tier 3 operator-page for invariant violations only. |
| **Build sub-ladder (E1–E6 — separate prompts)** | **E1 — `rebalance-planner` — LANDED 2026-06-24 at ACT-307.** **E2 — `order-submitter` — LANDED 2026-06-24 at ACT-310 (INC-77 closed same-PR).** **E3 — `state-machine` + `rejection-classifier` + `lifecycle-orchestrator` — LANDED 2026-06-24 at ACT-311** (entry + rank_exit only; short-stop deferred to DW-149 — both halves; defensive STOP guard installed). **E4 — `cache-propagator` + `cache-propagator-io` + MIG-119 — LANDED 2026-06-24 at ACT-312 (HYBRID resolution per DEC-068 clause e)** — operator-ratified after the §7-cache-existence probe found halted + transient-BP live verifiers are AUTHORITATIVE (a cache would INTRODUCE staleness per §2 axiom) while htb's prime ITSELF is stale (Alpaca `/v2/assets` shortable is a once-each-morning snapshot per Alpaca docs). Three NO-PAUSE classes: halted observability-emit ONLY (NO cache write — `failure_action='halt_cache_updated_from_rejection'` verbatim); transient-BP observability-emit ONLY (NO cache write — `failure_action='buying_power_cache_refreshed'` verbatim; the "refresh" is the next-tick live `verify_buying_power`); htb persisted-state record via MIG-119 `longshort_short_availability_cache` (`failure_action='short_availability_cache_updated_htb'` verbatim) — UPSERT on rejection, DELETE on genuine-success locate (`qty_available >= qty_requested`), partial locate (`qty_available < qty_requested`) does NOT clear. Composer↔verifier layering: classification rules transcribed VERBATIM from the `verify_*.ts` specs (`verify_halt_status` halted=true → fail; `verify_ssr_status` state≠not_active → fail; `verify_short_availability` !available OR qty_available<1 → fail; `verify_buying_power` available_bp<requested_total → ALL candidates fail with system-level reason). The composer DOES NOT call `reconcile()` — pre-flight gates run O(candidates × verifiers) per tick (up to 160 calls per rebalance) and would FLOOD `reconciliation_events` with PASS rows; the standalone `verify_*.ts` shells remain the AUTHORITATIVE reconciliation surface for post-fill / sweep contexts. Per §2 axiom (TYPED ABSENCE, NOT a synthetic SSR-clear sentinel) the composer accepts `ssrStatusFetcher?` as OPTIONAL — when absent, every SHORT candidate records `verifiers_skipped: ['verify_ssr_status']` in the composer's `skipped` Map AND the batch `summary.ssr_unavailable=true`. The candidate's `PreflightResult` reflects ONLY the verifiers that actually RAN — the composer does NOT synthesize an SSR-clear result, and it does NOT fabricate a failure where no signal exists. LONG candidates are unaffected (verify_ssr_status is short-side-only routing concern). **Phase-2 decision (operator)**: (a) inject an SSR fetcher from a non-Alpaca feed (Polygon / Tradier); (b) accept the degraded posture on paper v1 and document the typed absence in the operator runbook + the trigger's audit metadata; (c) defer short-side placement entirely until an SSR source is wired (long-only paper v1). Recommendation lands with the Phase-2 trigger build. The composer is structured to accept (a) without code change — Phase-2 just injects the fetcher into the trigger. |

### FP-055: Step A — Portfolio Construction / Target-Position Compute (longshort)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **ID** | FP-055 (next-free after FP-054). |
| **Status** | approved (operator-directed forward 2026-06-24 — Tier A; greenfield pure-compute upstream half of the paper-trading pipeline; ZERO execution surface, ZERO `longshort.execute` introduction, ZERO broker write, ZERO order). |
| **Problem** | The fallback combiner book lands daily (20L + 20S in `combiner_book`) but nothing downstream sizes it. The execution layer (DW-046) is DEC-gated and blocked until a sizing DEC ratifies (a) the binding of CROSSWIND §1.5 "current invested capital" to a broker field, (b) the per-name fraction, (c) partial-book policy, and (d) the v1 sizing model. The DEC needs OBSERVED numbers against the live book to lock those parameters — which requires the sizing kernel to exist FIRST as a pure-compute layer. |
| **Resolution** | Build a pure-compute portfolio-construction layer that reads `combiner_book` + `BrokerBuyingPower.account_equity` and writes per-ticker target-state rows to a new `longshort_target_positions` table. NO broker write. NO order. NO `longshort.execute`. Sizing kernel uses two named params: `allocation_pct` ∈ (0, 1] (D4, default 1.0, dashboard-settable) and `leverage` (D5, paper-locked at 1.0; Phase-8 DEC DW-137 unlocks). Math: `capital_base = equity × allocation_pct × leverage`; `per_name_notional = capital_base / book_size`. At full book (40) / allocation=1.0 / leverage=1.0 each name gets `equity / 40` (matches §1.5 verbatim — 2.5% per name, 100% gross, dollar-neutral). Decoupled `longshort.targets.published` audit event emitted after each successful UPSERT — the Step F sizing→execution trigger surface (analogous to `combiner.book_published`). |
| **Scope** | NEW: `supabase/migrations/…_longshort_target_positions.sql` (MIG-118) — table + RLS + GRANTs mirroring `combiner_book` verbatim; `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-targets/{target-position-builder,target-position-orchestrator,stub-capital-fetcher}.ts` + `…/target-position-builder_test.ts` (15 tests); `supabase/functions/longshort-targets-compute/index.ts` + `…-manual/index.ts`. EDIT: `docs/07-reference/{event-index.md,function-index.md,database-migration-ledger.md}`, `docs/08-planning/{feature-proposals.md,deferred-work-register.md}`, `docs/06-tracking/action-tracker.md`. |
| **Out of Scope** | (1) Order submission / broker write / POST /v2/orders. (2) `longshort.execute` permission key — explicitly NOT introduced (DEC-032 clause-4 preserved). (3) Live Alpaca capital-fetcher wiring — deferred to DW-138 (capital_source = `stub_100k` until ALPACA_PAPER_KEY/SECRET land). (4) Leverage > 1.0 — kernel ASSERTS leverage===1.0 and throws `LeverageLockViolationError` otherwise; Phase-8 DEC DW-137 is the sole authority to relax. (5) Cron arming — `longshort.targets.compute` is NOT registered in `job_registry` yet; arming is a follow-up after operator confirms the manual-fire dry-run. (6) Dashboard allocation control — Step A persists the param; the UI surface is a separate FP. (7) `target_shares` — left NULL at Step A (needs fill price; execution-layer concern). (8) §1.6 90–110% rebalance, §1.4 hysteresis/cap-25/31-day-re-entry block, position-delta computation — all execution-layer. (9) Sector-cap RE-enforcement — Step A WITNESSES (assert/audit per-(side,sector) ≤ 6); the cap is applied UPSTREAM at book assembly. |
| **Reference Impact** | event-index.md: 8 NEW events (`longshort.targets.compute.{started,completed,failed,skipped,manual_triggered,manual_completed,manual_failed}` + `longshort.targets.published`). function-index.md: 4 NEW entries (`target-position-builder`, `target-position-orchestrator`, `stub-capital-fetcher`, `longshort-targets-compute` + `-manual` edge fns). database-migration-ledger.md: NEW MIG-118. deferred-work-register.md: NEW DW-137 (Phase-8 leverage authorization DEC) + NEW DW-138 (Alpaca live capital-fetcher wiring). action-tracker.md: NEW ACT-302. No new permissions, configs, env-vars, or routes. No new npm dependency. |
| **Decision ID** | **DEC-067** (longshort v1 sizing model — landed 2026-06-24, `docs/decisions/DEC-067-longshort-sizing-model.md`). Ratifies the v1 sizing formula, the §1.5 capital-base rebinding (Rule 8 delta-carry), the `allocation_pct` parameter, the leverage paper-lock + column/kernel split (2.0 column ceiling provisionally ratified as DW-137 forward-compat bound), the partial-book policy, and the fallback-book-as-SIZING-INPUT acceptance. SIZING-ONLY — execution authorization deferred to the execution-layer DEC. DW-137 (Phase-8 leverage) is a SEPARATE downstream DEC governing supersession of CROSSWIND §1 L95 / L153 at the live-money boundary. |
| **DEC-deferred parameters (named, NOT hardcoded)** | D1 `sizing_basis` (recommend `'account_equity'`, only admitted value at Step A). D2 `per_name_pct` — derived: at full book, `1/book_size = 0.025` matches §1.5; allocation knob is the operator-facing surface, the literal is not configurable at Step A. D3 partial-book policy — kernel divides capital_base by actual book_size (no per-name re-distribution beyond the natural division; empty book = noop). D4 `allocation_pct` (∈ (0,1], default 1.0). D5 `leverage` (named param, paper-locked at 1.0 with hard assertion; Phase-8 DEC DW-137 unlocks). |
| **Verification** | (a) Migration MIG-118 applied — table + RLS + GRANTs landed; LIVE `read_query` confirms. (b) Kernel tests `deno test … target-position-builder_test.ts` → 15/15 PASS (8 property + 2 leverage-math + 1 leverage-lock + 2 allocation + 1 non-positive-equity + 1 Gate-6 wall-clock self-scan). (c) Live dry-run against `combiner_book` for `as_of=2026-06-23` with `account_equity=$100,000`, `allocation_pct=1.0`, `leverage=1.0`: 40 rows persisted (20L + 20S), each `target_notional = $2,500.00`, `gross_dollars per side = $50,000`, dollar-neutral, `capital_base = $100,000`, `ranker_source = count_normalized_fallback`. (d) `grep` confirms NO `POST /v2/orders` / `longshort.execute` / order / broker-write anywhere in Step A surface. (e) `grep` confirms `leverage === LEVERAGE_PAPER_LOCK` assertion present in kernel. (f) Both `deno.lock` files re-asserted at `version: 3`. |

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **ID** | FP-057 (next-free after FP-056). |

The cadence rebuild moved the strategy from once-daily-hardcoded (FP-052(a) interim) to intraday-configurable; the optimal cadence MEASUREMENT + LOCK is honestly deferred to Phase-7 per DEC-048's title invariant (executed via FP-058). |
| **Tier** | **A — financial-critical cadence-architecture money-path rebuild.** Performs the schema migration that underlies every combiner table + the planner rewrite that gains working-order visibility + the signal-cadence lift that moves the strategy from once-daily to intraday-configurable. |
| **Authority chain** | Operator-ratified [DEC-070](../decisions/DEC-070-longshort-cadence-rebuild.md) (this commit) — dual-independent investigation (Lovable + supervisor; prior turn) → reconciliation → operator merits-decision on the keystone (additive `intraday_slot` superset PK). [DEC-070](../decisions/DEC-070-longshort-cadence-rebuild.md) IMPLEMENTS DEC-048 (the cadence-as-config / Phase-7-measured principle) and RETIRES FP-052 disposition (a) only — the once-daily interim shortcut. Charter-first sequencing (inverse of FP-052 which was a built-then-deferred-the-cadence-piece interim) because the DEC ratifies the schema keystone the entire build branches on. |
| **Problem** | The once-daily strategy (FP-052 disposition (a) interim) runs but cannot react to intraday alpha decay — by spec (CROSSWIND §4.4) the strategy is designed for 50–80 ranking computations per trading day off 5 intraday-cadence signals (#1 analyst, #2 PEAD, #3 options-flow, #8 news, #9 catalyst). The interim hardcoded daily and deferred (i) the schema configurability that lets a single table hold both daily and intraday snapshots, (ii) the planner's working-order visibility (`rebalance-submit-orchestrator.ts:256-257` blind spot — would re-place over already-working orders under any sub-daily fire), (iii) the §11.0.7 #1 ranking-freshness gate (currently unwired), (iv) the combiner recompute trigger model, (v) the trainer-substrate intraday-slot dual-capture (which needs to start as PLUMBING ahead of data parity to avoid weeks of substrate-accrual delay later), (vi) the 5-signal cadence lift via the live FP-045 queue-worker engine, and (vii) the DW-163 dollar-neutrality aggregate's reframe from per-fire-pinned to rolling-window (per-fire pinning does not hold under 5-minute fires). [DEC-070](../decisions/DEC-070-longshort-cadence-rebuild.md) ratifies the reconciled architecture for all seven; FP-057 performs the build. |
| **Build sub-ladder (6 sub-steps — separate prompts; the ratified order from [DEC-070](../decisions/DEC-070-longshort-cadence-rebuild.md) build sequence)** | **Sub-step 1 — Schema keystone migration + substrate dual-capture plumbing (PARALLEL).** Performs [DEC-070](../decisions/DEC-070-longshort-cadence-rebuild.md) clauses (a) + (e). For each of `combiner_rankings` / `combiner_feature_vectors` / `combiner_book` / `combiner_book_shadow` / `combiner_forward_returns`: a single-transaction migration that ADDs `intraday_slot smallint NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`, POPULATES default 0 BEFORE the PK swap, ROTATES the PK from `(operator_id, …, as_of_date)` to `(operator_id, …, as_of_date, intraday_slot)`, re-creates every dependent FK + unique index against the rotated key, re-verifies RLS/GRANTs. Each migration is its own MIG-NNN ledger entry. Substrate dual-capture plumbing lands incrementally as each writer is updated to pass `intraday_slot` through (daily writers fix slot=0; intraday writers consume slot > 0 from the cadence-config parameter). Daily strategy keeps running on slot 0 throughout. **Sub-step 2 — Working-order visibility + ranking-freshness gate.** Performs [DEC-070](../decisions/DEC-070-longshort-cadence-rebuild.md) clauses (b) + (c). Planner gains `working_orders: InFlightOrder[]` input via the EXISTING `reconstructInFlight(ts)` path (already consumed by the advance-tick at `tick-scheduler.ts:86`); `rebalance-submit-orchestrator.ts` lines 256-257 blind spot consumes it; `effective_current = position_mv + Σ signed_working_notional`; partial-fill double-count test coverage. New `verify_ranking_freshness` verifier mirrors the existing `verify_quote_freshness` tolerance pattern; planner refuses to act on rankings older than N polling ticks (initial: 2 ticks × 5min = 10min). Closes DW-164; advances §11.0.7 #1. **Sub-step 3 — Combiner recompute trigger.** Performs [DEC-070](../decisions/DEC-070-longshort-cadence-rebuild.md) clause (d). The build-time sub-decision (event-triggered vs tick-poll-dirty-bit) lands here against the keystone-settled schema. **Sub-step 4 — Signal-cadence lift (5 signals, alpha-decay order).** Performs [DEC-070](../decisions/DEC-070-longshort-cadence-rebuild.md) clause (f). Lifts via the live FP-045 queue-worker engine in order: news (#8 5min) → catalyst (#9 5min) → analyst (#1 15min) → PEAD (#2 event-gated + 15min) → options-flow (#3 5min, VENDOR-BLOCKED on Tradier wall-time, lands after the DW-095-adjacent Polygon Options Advanced procurement OR active-flow subset decision). Cross-sectional (#6) + reversal (#7) stay daily-by-construction; insider (#4) + short-interest (#5) stay filing-cadence. **Sub-step 5 — DW-163 aggregate reframe.** Performs [DEC-070](../decisions/DEC-070-longshort-cadence-rebuild.md) clause (g). Per-fire pinning → rolling-window invariant; window + tolerance co-designed with the cadence parameter. Lands after Sub-step 2 so it has effective-current state to assert against. **Sub-step 6 — Promote intraday-trained combiner + Phase-7 cadence measurement + lock.** Performs DEC-048's "optimal cadence MEASURED at Phase 7, LOCKED before Phase 8" provision against the substrate the prior sub-steps land. This is the DEC-048 measurement step the FP-052(a) interim deferred. |
| **Considered and rejected alternatives (Constitution Rule 8 — preserved)** | (i) `as_of_run_id UUID` PK rotation instead of additive `intraday_slot` — rejected on bigger-bang migration risk + loss of natural ordering + horizon-arithmetic regression + non-free backward-compat; recorded in [DEC-070](../decisions/DEC-070-longshort-cadence-rebuild.md) clause (a). (ii) Clean-slate cancel-then-replace working-order model — rejected on ~1280 cancel/replace/session rate-limit-hostility + ROI bleed through repeated re-pricing; recorded in [DEC-070](../decisions/DEC-070-longshort-cadence-rebuild.md) clause (b). (iii) Pending-intent ledger — rejected on SURFACE-1 regression + compounding reconciliation cost; recorded in [DEC-070](../decisions/DEC-070-longshort-cadence-rebuild.md) clause (b). (iv) Full-recompute polling without dirty-bit — rejected as strictly dominated by tick-poll+dirty-bit; recorded in [DEC-070](../decisions/DEC-070-longshort-cadence-rebuild.md) clause (d). (v) Delta-on-changed-signals incremental combiner build — rejected on engineering cost; recorded in [DEC-070](../decisions/DEC-070-longshort-cadence-rebuild.md) clause (d). |
| **Operational continuity guarantee** | The once-daily strategy KEEPS RUNNING on `intraday_slot=0` throughout the rebuild. No cron disarm. Backward-compat from the additive keystone is the load-bearing invariant. |
| **STOP conditions for sub-step prompts** | (1) any sub-step that frames [DEC-070](../decisions/DEC-070-longshort-cadence-rebuild.md) as superseding DEC-048 → STOP. (2) any sub-step that diverges from the additive `intraday_slot` superset-key + single-transaction-per-table discipline → STOP. (3) any sub-step that disarms the once-daily cron → STOP. (4) any sub-step that omits one of the four rejected alternatives from its design memo → STOP. |

### FP-060: Assembler critical-signal-coverage precondition gate — refuse-to-fire on zero coverage (CHARTER STUB; AXIOM-SENSITIVE; build deferred)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **ID** | FP-060 (next-free after FP-058 in-file + FP-059 pre-reserved by ACT-365 for the "Signal Substrate Hardening" carrier). |
| **Status** | **CHARTERED 2026-06-29 at ACT-366 — STUB ONLY, build deferred.** Charter + scope + axiom-sensitivity flag only; the precondition-gate code is NOT written this turn. A separate authorized execution turn implements the gate against this charter. |
| **Tier** | **A — AXIOM-SENSITIVE.** Touches fail-loud→skip semantics in the combiner-assemble path. The gate MUST be a distinct skip-BEFORE-assemble path emitting a typed `outcome='skipped'` (reason `critical_signal_coverage_zero`), NOT a softening of the existing per-name fail-loud `missing_critical_signal_6` exclusion (which is correct and stays). §9 banned-pattern-adjacent / §2 axiom 3 typed-absence; Constitution Rule 11 HIGH at build time. |
| **Authority chain** | ACT-366 (this charter); the prior-turn signal-production outage diagnosis that surfaced the false-alarm failure mode at the 06-27 / 06-29 manual `longshort-combiner-assemble` fires; FP-057 RESOLVED at ACT-349 (the substrate the assemble runs against); §2 axiom 3 (typed-absence, not silent fabrication); §9 banned-patterns (no fail-loud softening). |
| **Problem** | When `longshort-combiner-assemble` is fired for a target `as_of_date` for which Critical Signal #6 (`cross_sectional_momentum_12_1`) — or any critical signal — has not yet computed, the existing per-name path correctly excludes every name with reason `missing_critical_signal_6` and persists ~839 feature-vectors-all-excluded. Downstream queries that count rows / observe coverage cannot distinguish this from a substrate outage; it produced an actionable-looking but false-alarm "signal-production outage" diagnosis last session at the manual 06-27 / 06-29 fires (the actual root cause was operator timing — manual fires ahead of scheduled signal production). The per-name exclusion is correct and must stay; what is missing is a precondition gate ABOVE it that refuses to fire at all when critical-signal coverage for the target date is zero, making the "wrong date / wrong time" failure mode VISIBLY DISTINCT from a substrate outage. |
| **Scope (when build is authorized)** | (a) A new precondition check at the head of `longshort-combiner-assemble` (BEFORE any per-name iteration) that counts critical-signal coverage for the target `(as_of_date, intraday_slot)` against the critical-signal set; (b) on `coverage == 0` the function emits a typed `outcome='skipped'` with `skip_reason='critical_signal_coverage_zero'` and `critical_signals_missing: [...]`, writes a `signal_compute_log` / `job_executions` row marking the skip, and returns WITHOUT writing any `combiner_feature_vectors` rows; (c) the existing per-name fail-loud `missing_critical_signal_6` exclusion is PRESERVED VERBATIM for the partial-coverage case (some names have the signal, some do not — that path stays); (d) telemetry: a counter for `assemble_skipped_critical_coverage_zero` distinguishable from `assemble_excluded_missing_critical_signal_6`; (e) a unit test that fires assemble against a date with zero critical-signal coverage and asserts the skip-without-write outcome; (f) a unit test that fires assemble against a date with partial critical-signal coverage and asserts the existing per-name exclusion still runs (REGRESSION GUARD against accidental fail-loud softening). |
| **AXIOM-SENSITIVITY FLAG (read before any build prompt)** | This FP modifies fail-loud→skip semantics in a money-path adjacent function. The build turn MUST: (i) preserve the existing per-name `missing_critical_signal_6` fail-loud exclusion unchanged — verified by diff; (ii) implement the precondition gate as a NEW skip path, not a modification of any existing exclusion path; (iii) emit `skipped` typed-outcome (NOT `succeeded` with zero rows — that re-creates the same masquerade in a new shape); (iv) carry a regression test that fires against partial-coverage and asserts the per-name exclusion still runs. Any build prompt that softens, replaces, or unifies the per-name exclusion with the precondition gate → STOP. Any build prompt that treats `succeeded(rows=0)` as equivalent to `skipped(reason=...)` → STOP. |
| **Considered and rejected at charter authoring** | (i) Procedural fix only (operator stops firing manual assembles ahead of signal production) — rejected because the failure mode is human-error-recurrent and the downstream-query masquerade survives any procedural fix; structural visibility is required. (ii) Softening the per-name `missing_critical_signal_6` exclusion to write `is_present=false` rows without excluding — rejected on §2 axiom 3 (silent fabrication) + §9 banned-patterns (fail-loud softening); the per-name path is correct and must stay. (iii) Refactoring the assemble path to merge the precondition gate INTO the per-name iteration — rejected because the two paths emit different typed outcomes (`skipped` vs `excluded`) and must remain distinguishable for downstream coverage queries. |
| **Preconditions for build authorization** | (1) The critical-signal set must be explicitly enumerated in the charter at build time (currently #6 `cross_sectional_momentum_12_1` is named; the full set must be confirmed against the per-name exclusion logic before the precondition gate is written so the two paths agree on the same critical-signal definition). (2) The build turn must run BEFORE any cadence lift under build-to-paper Step 4a (the gate is more valuable on the manual-fire / off-cadence failure mode that the lift will introduce more surface for). |
| **STOP conditions for activation prompts** | (1) any activation prompt that softens or replaces the existing per-name `missing_critical_signal_6` fail-loud exclusion → STOP. (2) any activation prompt that emits `succeeded(rows=0)` instead of `skipped(reason=critical_signal_coverage_zero)` → STOP. (3) any activation prompt that lacks the partial-coverage regression-guard test → STOP. (4) any activation prompt that defines the critical-signal set differently from the per-name exclusion logic → STOP. |
| **References** | ACT-366 (this charter); the prior-turn signal-production outage diagnosis (the false-alarm failure mode this gate prevents); FP-057 RESOLVED at ACT-349 (the substrate); `docs/08-planning/phase-status-ledger.md` Part 5 Step 4a (the cadence-lift surface the gate de-risks); §2 axiom 3 + §9 banned-patterns (the typed-absence + fail-loud invariants the build turn MUST honor); the per-name `missing_critical_signal_6` exclusion in `longshort-combiner-assemble` (the path that STAYS unchanged). |
