Authoring later execution turns MAY parallelize 4M.2 / 4M.3 / 4M.4 if engineering throughput permits; 4M.1 MUST land first and 4M.5 MUST land last. |
| **BINDING DECISION — DW-160 schema = column-on-lots** | Add `settlement_state` + `expected_settlement_ts` to `longshort_lots` in 4M.1's migration; do NOT create a sibling `longshort_settlement_events` table. **Rationale:** (i) matches `verify_settlement_status` 1:1 — the verifier surface has no aggregation key, no `settlement_id`, no many-lots-to-one-settlement contract (per `verify_settlement_status.ts` grep this session); (ii) T+1 is per-fill in US cash equities — no multi-fill-settles-together case exists on the Phase-4 → Phase-7 money path; (iii) atomic with the open-writer — one fill event, one row, one transaction, no FK race; (iv) smaller migration + blast radius. **Reversibility ADR (forward-pointer):** if Phase-7+ surfaces a real many-to-one settlement event (e.g., merger cash components forcing re-settlement), the upgrade to a sibling `longshort_settlement_events(lot_id, ...)` table is mechanical: copy the two columns into the new table, leave them on `longshort_lots` as deprecated denormalization for one release, then drop. The re-settlement audit trail is recoverable from `reconciliation_events` meanwhile (the verifier-firing log). This decision is REVERSIBLE and the upgrade path is documented. |
| **BINDING — SOFT PHASE-6 DEPENDENCY (precedent: FP-057's verify_rebalance_aggregate)** | The 5 verifier reader-wires complete IN Phase-4 (each verifier reads its internal table). But the verifier broker-side COMPARISON only becomes meaningful once DW-058 ships the real Alpaca / Polygon broker-side fetchers under Phase-6. Until then, the Phase-4 verifiers fire against the EXISTING mock-fetcher path with an explicit TODO marker pointing at DW-058 — **same pattern FP-057 used for `verify_rebalance_aggregate`** when the broker-truth fetcher was not yet wired. Cite that precedent in each sub-step build prompt so the soft dependency is not mistaken for a Phase-4 blocker. |
| **§7 pre-flight composer plug-ins** | TWO BLOCK plug-ins land under this FP (both modify §7 pre-flight composer behavior): (1) **30-day conditional-loss BLOCK** under 4M.3 — when a candidate symbol has a `wash_sale_events` row with `status='blocking'` and `block_until > now()`, the §7 composer emits a BLOCK with reason `wash_sale_block`. (2) **Unapplied-CA BLOCK** under 4M.4 — when a candidate symbol has any `corporate_actions` row with `ex_date <= today` not yet applied to its open lots, the §7 composer emits a BLOCK with reason `unapplied_corporate_action`. Each BLOCK is wired through the existing composer surface; no new pre-flight architecture. |
| **Acceptance criteria (per sub-step; for the build turns)** | 4M.1: lots ledger migration applied + ledgered as MIG-NNN; open-writer fires on AlpacaFillFetcher result + writes a lot row with `settlement_state='pending'` + `expected_settlement_ts=trade_ts+1bd`; close-writer fires on exit + emits closed-lot list; `verify_lot_record` reader returns the table row; unit test covers fill→open→close path. 4M.2: T+1 reconciler runs daily + flips `settlement_state='settled'`; §7 BP read distinguishes settled-vs-unsettled cash (PDT flag aware); `verify_settlement_status` reader returns column projection. 4M.3: `wash_sale_events` migration + RLS / GRANT; Path-A + Path-B + §7.9 writers exercised; §7 composer emits `wash_sale_block` for blocking rows; `verify_wash_sale_record` reader returns the row. 4M.4: Polygon CA adapter produces splits / divs / mergers / spin-offs at parity with the existing `polygon-quote-fetcher.ts` adapter shape; `corporate_actions` store written; ex-date applier mutates open lots correctly (2:1 split → qty×2, cost_basis÷2); §7 composer emits `unapplied_corporate_action` BLOCK; `verify_corporate_action_clean` reader returns clean (incl. AC-26 `broker_basis_adjusted` reconciliation case). 4M.5: `realized_pnl` migration; writer fires per-closed-lot from 4M.1's close-writer; net-PnL consults 4M.3's wash-sale adjustment; `verify_realized_pnl` reader returns the row. ACROSS ALL SUB-STEPS: GRANTs present per project rule; RLS enabled + policies same migration (D1); idempotent migrations (D3); per-strategy scoping (D4); MIG-NNN ledgered (D5). |
| **STOP conditions for activation prompts** | (1) Any activation prompt that builds verifiers from scratch (they exist) → STOP. (2) Any activation prompt that builds real broker-side fetchers (those are DW-058 / Phase-6) → STOP. (3) Any activation prompt that repurposes the Tradier CA fetcher (signal-#9-reserved per `tradier-corporate-actions-fetcher.ts:2` / FP-049 / DEC-057) → STOP. (4) Any activation prompt that creates a sibling `longshort_settlement_events` table for DW-160 instead of column-on-lots → STOP (the binding decision above must be honored; reversal is a separate governance turn). (5) Any activation prompt that softens the §7 composer's existing fail-loud behavior (replaces a BLOCK with a warning, etc.) → STOP. (6) Any activation prompt that follows DW numbering (157 → 158 → 159 → 160 → 161) instead of the 158-foundational / 159-terminal coupling order → STOP. (7) Any activation prompt that wires `verify_realized_pnl` BEFORE 4M.3 closes (net-PnL depends on wash-sale adjustment) → STOP. |

Their next discharge is gated on either live-broker (DW-160 / DW-196) or a new vendor / FILL-aggregate cross-check DW — NOT on more paper work. **Gap surfaced (not invented this turn per directive):** `verifyCorporateActionClean` has no live orchestration caller yet (only `longshort-verifiers/index.ts` re-export + test caller); bootstrap exposure + the `corporateActionFetcher` interface slot ARE the plumbing 6I.2b ships — the orchestration call-site wire (`interfaces.corporateActionFetcher ?? createInternalCorporateActionStatusFetcher()` fallback pattern) is a separate sub-step to land with the broader verifier tick wiring. **6I.2b is therefore PARTIALLY-EXECUTED**: the single-honest seam shipped + the 3 sibling seams formally re-affirmed STAY-SOFT in the ledger; the sub-step does NOT close (its closure waits on the structurally-unavailable seams' live-broker discharge under FP-062 successor turns).

#### FP-062-ADD-02 (Rule 8 additive, appended 2026-06-29 at ACT-385)

**Governance correction — charter-vs-repo discrepancy on the DW-060 re-enablement chain; 6I.3a ALREADY-SATISFIED at FP-008.4 Commit 9; 6I.3b re-scoped with the atomic-ordering AC.** Originating from the FP-062 6I.3a grounding investigation (same turn as ACT-385): the two-invocation liveness rule was BUILT + TESTED + SEEDED before this charter was authored, and the charter's ladder + DW-homing + conditions cells are internally inconsistent on the 6I.3a-vs-6I.3b mapping. Originals above are PRESERVED VERBATIM per Constitution Rule 8; this block SUPERSEDES the cited cells by pointer.

**Amendment 1 — 6I.3a (two-invocation liveness rule, DW-060 condition 2) is ALREADY-SATISFIED, NOT a build.** The ladder cell's "6I.3a — implement FP-008.4 #11 in `longshort-reconciliation-tick/index.ts`" is **STALE**. The rule was implemented at **FP-008.4 Commit 9** as a SEPARATE edge function — `supabase/functions/longshort-reconciliation-liveness-check/index.ts` (242 lines: the exact two-consecutive-zero-`fetcher_source='live'`-row predicate over the last 2 completed `longshort.reconciliation_periodic_sweep` executions on `call_name IN ('verify_buying_power','verify_position','verify_universe_membership')`; 2-rung STOP ladder: rung (a) `reconcile()`-written `outcome='system_bug'` event row, rung (c) `UPDATE public.job_registry SET enabled=false WHERE id='longshort.reconciliation_periodic_sweep'`; rung (b) alert-emit deliberately omitted = DW-086 deferral). Test coverage in the sibling `index_test.ts` (7 Deno.test cases) covers the charter's AC verbatim: "POSITIVE: two consecutive empty windows → STOP" + "NEGATIVE: a single empty tick is not a STOP — the rule requires two consecutive empties" + insufficient-history + live-rows-observed + scope-limited-to-2-most-recent. Provenance scoping (`'replay'`, `'universe_cross_check'`, `'unknown'` EXCLUDED from the predicate) precisely matches the three `fetcher_source='live'` tags 6I.2a-remaining flipped (ACT-384) — the two pieces compose by construction. **MIG-059** added `reconciliation_events.fetcher_source` (the INC-40 schema-column requirement that "real broker, zero positions" vs "mock, returned null" can't be distinguished by pure SQL otherwise), backfilled `'unknown'`, and SEEDED `job_registry` row `'longshort.reconciliation_liveness_check'` with `enabled=false`, `schedule='*/10 * * * *'` — with the binding atomic-ordering note (liveness-check armed FIRST). **6I.3a is RE-SCOPED to VERIFY-ONLY**: confirm the Commit-9 fn + 7-test suite + MIG-059 seed satisfy DW-060 condition (2); no build. Condition (2) is **SATISFIED**.

**Amendment 2 — resolve the internal 6I.3a-vs-6I.3b mapping inconsistency (CANONICAL).** The original "THREE re-enablement conditions" cell says condition (2) is "delivered by 6I.3b below; NOT YET LANDED at this charter"; the ladder cell + DW-homing cell attribute the liveness rule to 6I.3a; the conditions cell attributes the re-enable migration to 6I.3c. Both are stale. **Canonical mapping going forward (SUPERSEDES the conditions-cell numbering):** **6I.3a** = two-invocation liveness rule (**ALREADY-SATISFIED**, Commit 9 / liveness-check fn + tests + MIG-059); **6I.3b** = the DW-060 re-enable migration (OPEN — see Amendment 3); **6I.3c** = cron arming / activation verification (OPEN, gated on 6I.3b). The "NOT YET LANDED at this charter" annotation on the liveness rule is **SUPERSEDED** (it landed at Commit 9, BEFORE the charter was authored).

**Amendment 3 — 6I.3b re-scoped with the atomic-ordering AC (safety-critical; elevated from docstring to charter).** The DW-060 re-enable migration (6I.3b) MUST, in a SINGLE migration, in this exact order: (1) `UPDATE public.job_registry SET enabled=true WHERE id='longshort.reconciliation_liveness_check'` (the watcher armed FIRST), THEN (2) `UPDATE public.job_registry SET enabled=true WHERE id='longshort.reconciliation_periodic_sweep'` (the sweep). This atomic-ordering invariant — **liveness-check armed BEFORE the sweep can fire** — is elevated from `supabase/functions/longshort-reconciliation-liveness-check/index.ts` lines 7-8 verbatim ("MUST first set this job's enabled=true (atomic ordering: liveness-check armed BEFORE the sweep is allowed to fire — pre-empts an INC-39-class seam reopening)") to a CHARTER-LEVEL 6I.3b AC. The migration MUST: (i) cite **MIG-058** (the original sweep disarm); (ii) confirm condition (1) landed (6I.2a-remaining / **ACT-384** — `MOCK_BP_FETCHER`/`MOCK_POSITION_FETCHER` replaced by `createLiveBrokerInterfaces()` in the tick handler; three `fetcher_source='live'` tags); (iii) confirm condition (2) landed (Commit 9 fn + **MIG-059** seed). **Exact `job_registry.id` literals (grep-verified per Catalog #50, NOT display names):** `'longshort.reconciliation_liveness_check'` and `'longshort.reconciliation_periodic_sweep'`. Reverse order (sweep armed before watcher) is a STOP-condition that re-opens the INC-39-class seam the MIG-058 disarm exists to prevent.

**Amendment 4 — condition (1) location correction.** The original conditions cell says condition (1) is "delivered by 6I.2a below". Clarify: 6I.2a was executed in TWO halves — the broker-module de-dup (**ACT-381**, the `src/` tree deletion + harness move) AND the handler mock-replacement (6I.2a-remaining / **ACT-384**, replacing `MOCK_BP_FETCHER` + `MOCK_POSITION_FETCHER` in `supabase/functions/longshort-reconciliation-tick/index.ts` with `createLiveBrokerInterfaces()` and flipping all three `fetcher_source` tags `'mock'`→`'live'`). Condition (1) [real fetchers replacing the mocks in the tick handler] was delivered by the **6I.2a-remaining half (ACT-384)**, NOT the de-dup half. Both halves are now complete.

**DW-060 chain status (post-ADD-02):** condition (1) ✓ ACT-384 (6I.2a-remaining); condition (2) ✓ FP-008.4 Commit 9 + MIG-059 (per Amendment 1); condition (3) **OPEN** — 6I.3b re-enable migration (per Amendment 3) → then 6I.3c cron-arming verification. The `longshort.reconciliation_periodic_sweep` cron remains correctly **DISARMED** (MIG-058 in effect) until 6I.3b lands.

#### FP-062-ADD-03 (Rule 8 additive, appended 2026-06-29 at ACT-390)

**Governance correction — the 6I.3c ladder cell silently assumed the reconciliation handlers were cron-invocable when they were not; records the handler-auth prerequisite (6I.3c-pre, BUILT at ACT-389) and the cron-only split-handler pattern decision.** Originating from the FP-062 6I.3c grounding investigation that preceded ACT-389: the charter's 6I.3c cell ("activate the pg_cron schedule for the periodic sweep; verify by observing real verify rows in `reconciliation_events` across two consecutive ticks") quietly presumed `longshort-reconciliation-tick` + `longshort-reconciliation-liveness-check` accepted the `CRON_SECRET` Bearer (`X-Cron-Secret`) header pg_cron actually sends. They did NOT: both authenticated via `authenticateRequest` + `checkPermissionOrThrow` (user-JWT — tick required `'longshort.view'`, liveness-check required `'longshort.manage'`), and the cron-secret path was unimplemented. The mismatch was masked by MIG-058's disarm — neither handler had ever been pg_cron-fired in production; both were exclusively operator-UI-invoked with user JWTs (historical) or service-role-internal. A naive 6I.3c (arming the cron without first converting the auth) would have been the FIRST pg_cron invocation of either handler → arm → fire → 401 (no JWT in `CRON_SECRET` Bearer) → zero `fetcher_source='live'` verify rows produced → the two-invocation liveness rule reads two-consecutive-empty → self-disarm via rung-(c) (a silent false-DEFECT). Nothing regressed in code; the invocation source changed. Originals above are PRESERVED VERBATIM per Constitution Rule 8; this block SUPERSEDES the cited cell by pointer.

**Amendment 1 — 6I.3c ladder cell omitted the handler-auth prerequisite (CHARTER-vs-REPO).** The original ladder cell `"6I.3c — DW-060 cron arming: activate the pg_cron schedule for the periodic sweep; verify by observing real verify rows in reconciliation_events across two consecutive ticks"` and the canonical mapping carried into FP-062-ADD-02 Amendment 2 (`6I.3c = cron arming / activation verification`) both silently assumed cron-invocability of the two reconciliation handlers. They were authored as user-JWT handlers (`authenticateRequest` + `checkPermissionOrThrow` — `longshort.view` for the tick, `longshort.manage` for the liveness-check) and pg_cron's only credential is the `CRON_SECRET` Bearer via the `X-Cron-Secret` header — `authenticateRequest` does not recognize it. The omission is real (the cell shipped without naming the prerequisite); the failure mode is the silent false-DEFECT pathway described in the header (arm → 401 → zero live verify rows → liveness rule self-disarms). The cell is SUPERSEDED by Amendment 4 below; the prerequisite is recorded in Amendment 2; the pattern choice is recorded in Amendment 3.

**Amendment 2 — 6I.3c-pre (handler-auth prerequisite) inserted and BUILT at ACT-389.** A new sub-step `6I.3c-pre — convert both reconciliation handlers to cron-only auth` was inserted ahead of 6I.3c and BUILT in the same authorized turn (ACT-389, HEAD 252a152b). Both `supabase/functions/longshort-reconciliation-tick/index.ts` and `supabase/functions/longshort-reconciliation-liveness-check/index.ts` were converted to cron-only `verifyCronSecret(req)` (mirroring `supabase/functions/longshort-universe-quarterly-refresh` exactly): `const cronAuthError = verifyCronSecret(req); if (cronAuthError) return cronAuthError;`. The `authenticateRequest` + `checkPermissionOrThrow` imports + call-sites + the user-JWT `ctx` were removed; `correlationId` was re-sourced as a handler-local `crypto.randomUUID()` (the reconcile-callback `ctx.ts` inside the liveness-check's `failure_action` is a DIFFERENT `ctx` — the lifecycle callback ctx, threaded from the handler's `productionClock` — and was correctly left untouched). Pure code, no migration, no firing. 13/13 Deno tests pass on both handlers (pure classifier/predicate suites — no auth-sentinel updates required). DW-060 handler-auth prerequisite SATISFIED.

**Amendment 3 — pattern decision: cron-only (split precedent), NOT dual-auth-in-one, NOT a manual sibling — with rationale.** Three options were considered for the auth conversion:
- **Option A (rejected) — dual-auth-in-one** (one handler accepts both `CRON_SECRET` Bearer AND user JWT, branching on header presence): rejected for this Tier-A safety-critical class. Widens the auth surface for a caller that does not exist. Correctness subtlety: `verifyCronSecret` in `supabase/functions/_shared/cron-auth.ts` returns a 401 `Response` when `X-Cron-Secret` is absent — a literal "verifyCronSecret first, user-JWT fallback" would 401 a UI caller before the fallback could run; making it work would require header-presence gating, not unconditional-first. Moot here (no UI caller), but recorded as the structural reason dual-auth-in-one would have needed care.
- **Option B (rejected) — cron-only handler + manual user-JWT sibling** (the canonical split, e.g. `longshort-universe-quarterly-refresh` + `longshort-universe-manual-quarterly-refresh`): not needed because there is no caller to preserve. Grounding established NO live UI caller for either handler — every `longshort-reconciliation-tick` reference in `src/` + edge fns is doc/comment-only (no `functions.invoke`, no `fetch`, no admin "Run reconciliation now" button); `longshort-reconciliation-liveness-check` is self-contained per its own header ("No external invoke — the observed is the verdict itself"). With no caller, the manual sibling would be dead code.
- **Option C (adopted) — cron-only on both handlers** (the chosen pattern): mirrors the `quarterly-refresh` cron handler exactly; keeps the Tier-A reconciliation handlers' auth surface minimal (one path, one failure mode); the two handlers are now symmetric. Halt-authority remains enforced by pg_cron-only invocation discipline (the `CRON_SECRET` IS the authorization, as in `quarterly-refresh`). If a UI caller is ever required, a `manual-reconciliation-*` sibling can be added then — the operator turn that needs it is the right time to weigh the surface widening, not now.

**Amendment 4 — 6I.3c (the cron.schedule) is now unblocked and re-scoped.** With 6I.3c-pre landed, 6I.3c is now: an operator-applied `sql/NN_longshort_reconciliation_cron_schedule.sql` migration (per §22.5.3 — `YOUR_CRON_SECRET_VALUE` placeholder substituted out-of-band by the operator, the substituted value NEVER committed) registering BOTH cron schedules with the `X-Cron-Secret` Bearer header the handlers now accept, in this exact order — **watcher (`longshort.reconciliation_liveness_check`) cron registered FIRST, then sweep (`longshort.reconciliation_periodic_sweep`) cron registered SECOND** (mirroring the watcher-first ordering FP-062-ADD-02 Amendment 3 elevated to charter-level for the arming axis, here mirrored for the firing axis). **Verification AC (non-synchronous — requires actual schedule windows):** observe two consecutive `*/5` sweep ticks each producing non-zero `fetcher_source='live'` rows in `public.reconciliation_events` (`call_name IN ('verify_buying_power','verify_position','verify_universe_membership')`) before declaring 6I.3c discharged. The liveness-check's two-consecutive-empty STOP rule is the structural backstop if the firing is not actually producing live observations.

**DW-060 chain status (post-ADD-03):** condition (1) ✓ ACT-384 (real `createLiveBrokerInterfaces` in tick handler); condition (2) ✓ FP-008.4 Commit 9 + MIG-059 (liveness rule + tests + `fetcher_source` column + liveness-check job seed); condition (3) ✓ MIG-145 / ACT-387 (arming axis — both `job_registry` rows `enabled=true`, watcher-first); **handler-auth prerequisite ✓ ACT-389 (6I.3c-pre — cron-only `verifyCronSecret` on both handlers)**; firing = 6I.3c (operator-applied `cron.schedule` SQL, watcher-first, §22.5.3 placeholder, two-tick observation AC) — PENDING.

**Cross-references:** ACT-389 (6I.3c-pre BUILT — the auth conversion); ACT-390 (this governance authoring); ACT-387 / MIG-145 (6I.3b arming axis); ACT-384 (condition 1 — real fetchers); FP-008.4 Commit 9 + MIG-059 (condition 2); MIG-058 (the original disarm); `supabase/functions/longshort-reconciliation-tick/index.ts` + `longshort-reconciliation-liveness-check/index.ts` (the two converted handlers); `supabase/functions/longshort-universe-quarterly-refresh` (the cron-only mirror precedent); `supabase/functions/longshort-universe-manual-quarterly-refresh` (the split-precedent manual sibling — not adopted here, no caller to preserve); `supabase/functions/_shared/cron-auth.ts` `verifyCronSecret` (the auth primitive; 401-on-missing-header semantics noted in Amendment 3); FP-062-ADD-02 (the prior charter-vs-repo correction this mirrors in shape; canonical 6I.3a/b/c mapping); DW-060 (the chain reconciled); INC-39 (the seam class watcher-first ordering protects, here mirrored on the firing axis); §22.5.3 (operator-applied `YOUR_CRON_SECRET_VALUE` placeholder discipline); Catalog #50 (exact-literal `job_registry.id` discipline); Constitution Rule 8 (this block is additive; originals preserved verbatim).

**Falsification path (recorded so the deferral does not ossify in either direction):** if the NEXT post-EOD rebalance cycle ALSO produces zero fills, the finding sharpens — it is NOT a cadence-vintage gap but a deeper book-construction / submission defect, and DW-203 B2 (morning-vintage refinement) is STILL not the answer; that path triggers a fresh diagnosis turn, not a B2 charter. If the next post-EOD cycle produces fills and post-attribution analysis (FP-065 / the ACT-419 snapshot lineage) later shows the morning-vintage gap costs ≥ a material bp threshold (threshold itself set at first-attribution time, since it cannot be set without any fill data), DW-203 B2 (morning-vintage refinement) gets chartered THEN — with the cost number in its justification.

**Cross-references:** ACT-425 (this verdict-correction authoring); ACT-419 (the ranking-snapshot sidecar deployed today, un-exercised by the empty-book run); ACT-418 (the EXIT-GATE ATTESTATION cell this corrects the framing for); ACT-403 (fill-accounting path — DEPLOYED but un-exercised end-to-end); ACT-407 / ACT-408 / DEC-070 clause (i) (the DW-203 B1 Gate-3 fix that fired correctly today); FP-064 / DEC-068 clause (s) / ACT-416 (the unrelated DW-058 B2 disposition — naming-collision guard); FP-065 (post-window full §1.1 entry-attribution — supplies the substrate for the morning-vintage cost measurement the DW-203 B2 re-arm trigger requires); DW-203 + DW-203's Rule-8 note (appended this turn in `deferred-work-register.md`); DW-201 (short-skew re-eval — not measurable today); DW-202 (pager — quiet); DW-138 (the BP-escalation deferred class); DW-204 (shadow-rank overlap — evidence-accrual co-ride); Constitution Rule 8 (this block is additive; originals preserved verbatim).

### FP-063: Ranker tiebreaker symmetry remediation — asymmetric short-side tiebreaker on BOTH live + shadow rankers (CHARTER STUB; MONEY-PATH; build deferred pending DW-204 overlap-frequency evidence)

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| **Status** | REGISTERED — build deferred. Authored 2026-06-30 at ACT-410 alongside the DW-204 fix (b) containment ship. |
| **Tier** | A — MONEY-PATH (alters live `short_rank` on tied composite-score names → can change which names get seeded into the live book on tie days; ROI surface non-zero). |
| **Mode** | DOCUMENTATION-ONLY at registration. Build mode + execution branch gated on the evidence-window outcome (see Evidence Window below). |
| **Origin** | Reconciled dual-investigation (operator + Lovable) on DW-204. Lovable's grep-grounded root-cause (shadow-ranker.ts:188-189 long: `adjusted DESC, ticker ASC`; shadow-ranker.ts:197-198 short: `adjusted ASC, ticker ASC`; same `ticker ASC` tiebreaker on both ends) supersedes the operator's earlier "degenerate composite score" framing. Operator's grep verification surfaced that the LIVE ranker (`ranker.ts:190-205`) has the IDENTICAL symmetric tiebreaker — making the structural fix a money-path change on BOTH surfaces, not a shadow-only fix. |
| **Problem** | Both the live ranker and the shadow ranker sort long/short with a SYMMETRIC `ticker ASC` tiebreaker. When the primary sort key (live: `composite`; shadow: `adjusted`) has a tied block straddling the `BOOK_SEED_SIZE=20` seed cut, the same alphabetically-earliest tickers within that tied block are pulled into the top-20 seeds for BOTH sides → both-sides overlap. Today this manifests in the no-exclusion shadow (DW-204): HIMS / QCOM / SMCI / SNDK on 2026-06-29 are the alphabetically-earliest survivors of a mid-distribution tied block (likely `adjusted=0` under a `no_gate` variant or shrinkage-collapsed `*_k10`). The live ranker only escapes today because critical-coverage exclusion keeps its pool small enough that the tied-block-straddling-cut condition rarely fires — but the bug is structurally identical and the same time bomb. |
| **Fix Shape** | Apply an asymmetric tiebreaker (short side → `ticker DESC` instead of `ticker ASC`) to BOTH the live and shadow rankers, in lockstep. Long sort stays `<primary> DESC, ticker ASC`; short sort becomes `<primary> ASC, ticker DESC`. Result: a tied primary-key block at the seed cut now surfaces alphabetically-earliest tickers on the long side and alphabetically-LATEST tickers on the short side — no overlap from the symmetry root. |
| **Invariant Preserved (load-bearing)** | DEC-059 `gated_k0 ≡ live_gated` byte-identity (shadow-ranker.ts:27-30 docstring; `shadow-ranker_test.ts:210` REGRESSION TIE — `ACT-242` proved 40/40 byte-identical). Applying the asymmetric tiebreaker to BOTH live AND shadow in the SAME commit preserves the invariant: both surfaces now use `short → ticker DESC`, so the gated_k0 shadow continues to mirror live_gated byte-for-byte on tied composite names. A shadow-only fix would BREAK this invariant and is a STOP-condition (Lovable's original fix (a) shape was caught and corrected at reconciliation — see ACT-409 / ACT-410). |
| **Why deferred (NOT bundled into DW-204 ACT-410)** | The DW-204 ship is blast-radius containment (per-variant skip-on-overlap), strictly shadow-only, zero invariant risk, zero money-path touch — it un-stalls the Phase-3.3 T+10 label substrate (0/12 → ≥1/12 variants accrue) TODAY. FP-063 is the structural fix and deserves its own FP with its own ROI evaluation of the live-book seeding impact on tie days. Bundling them would be "helpful refactoring while here" (forbidden by scope discipline) and would compress two decisions — an urgent substrate-unblock and a money-path structural change — into one under time pressure. Sequencing: ship (b) → observe overlap frequency for ~1 week → author FP-063 build branch with the frequency data as ROI evidence. |
| **Evidence Window (build gate)** | The DW-204 fix (b) writes `variants_skipped_overlap` to the `longshort.combiner.shadow_rank.completed` / `.failed` audit metadata on every fire (cron + manual). Each entry carries the variant name, inclusion_rule, k, and the overlapping ticker set. ≥1 week of accruing this telemetry feeds: (i) overlap frequency per-variant (does the symmetry root actually fire often, or is the no_gate fixture a corner case?); (ii) the ticker set churn (Lovable's prediction is the set changes day-to-day as the composite churns — verifies the tiebreaker root vs an alternative explanation); (iii) any signal of live-side risk (none expected, since the live ranker excludes critical-coverage failures — but the data will show if a tied block ever reaches the live seed cut). The build branch opens once the evidence supports proceeding (or rules out alternative root causes). |
| **Acceptance Criteria (when build branch opens)** | (1) `ranker.ts` short-side comparator changed to `<composite> ASC, ticker DESC` in the SAME commit as `shadow-ranker.ts` short-side comparator changed to `<adjusted> ASC, ticker DESC` (single atomic change — partial application breaks DEC-059). (2) The `gated_k0` byte-identity regression-tie test (`shadow-ranker_test.ts:210`) is REGENERATED against the new asymmetric ordering — it must still assert byte-identical (ticker, long_rank, short_rank) between shadow_gated_k0 and live computeRankings on fully-gated input, just under the new tiebreaker. (3) Live `compute_rankings_test.ts` (or equivalent) gets a NEW assertion that a tied composite block straddling the seed cut produces NON-overlapping long/short seeds (the bug's regression test). (4) Operator-acknowledged ROI evaluation of the live-book-seeding impact on tied composite names (sample from the DW-204 evidence window applied retroactively to the live composite distribution). (5) DW-204 evidence-window data archived in the FP-063 build artifact set. |
| **Constraints (STOP-conditions)** | A shadow-only asymmetric-tiebreaker fix → STOP (breaks DEC-059). Applying the fix to one ranker but not the other in the same commit → STOP (breaks DEC-059 mid-commit). Re-introducing exclusion into the shadow's no-exclusion variants to "avoid" the overlap → STOP (defeats the measurement intent of the shadow). Changing the long-side tiebreaker as well (making BOTH sides DESC, or both ASC) → STOP (just shifts the same bug to a different alphabetical bucket — must be ASYMMETRIC between sides, not symmetrically inverted). |
| **Cross-references** | DW-204 (the immediate substrate stall this FP structurally remediates); ACT-409 (DW-204 registration + dual-investigation Part 2); ACT-410 (DW-204 fix (b) ship + this FP registration); DEC-059 (the gated_k0 ≡ live_gated byte-identity invariant the fix must preserve); `ranker.ts:190-205` (live ranker symmetric tiebreaker — co-fix target); `shadow-ranker.ts:188-189 / 197-198` (shadow ranker symmetric tiebreaker — co-fix target); `shadow-ranker.ts:27-30` (the invariant docstring); `shadow-ranker_test.ts:210` (REGRESSION TIE test — must be regenerated under the new ordering); ACT-242 (40/40 byte-identical proof — baseline for the regeneration); `shadow-ranker-orchestrator.ts` lines 254-end (the DW-204 per-variant skip + `variants_skipped_overlap` evidence stream that gates this FP's build branch); BOOK_SEED_SIZE = 20 (`ranker-constants.ts`); CROSSWIND §10.M (the Phase-3.3 T+10 label substrate this protects). |

### FP-064: Pre-live halt-coverage — Polygon Advanced WS condition-code halt fetcher (CHARTER STUB; MONEY-PATH; build deferred to gap-(e) discharge; LIVE-FIRE RATIFICATION BLOCKER)

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| **Status** | REGISTERED — build deferred. Authored 2026-06-30 at ACT-416 concurrent with DEC-068 clause (s) (paper-v1 halt typed-absence posture) and DW-058-B2-ADD-02 (procurement-framing correction). |
| **Tier** | **A — MONEY-PATH (live-order safety control).** Halts affect both sides of the book; absent a real halt feed, live placements can enter / fail-to-exit halted names. Clause (s) GUARDRAIL 3 names FP-064 closure as a hard prerequisite for the live-money DEC. |
| **Mode** | DOCUMENTATION-ONLY at registration. Build mode opens at FP-062 gap-(e) discharge (first end-to-end live-fills validation observed); WS-probe-first Step A is the first build action. |
| **Origin** | Reconciled framing-correction on DW-058-B2. The ACT-400 (FP-062 sub-step 6I.1b) REST-only probe concluded "Phase-7 / tier-upgrade-gated CONFIRMED." That conclusion is REST-only-probe-scoped and is FALSE as a procurement claim: (i) Polygon ships NO dedicated halt-boolean REST field on ANY tier; (ii) we already hold Stocks Advanced (DEC-068 clause (r) line 635; consumed at `broker-bootstrap.ts:246` for NBBO today); (iii) the canonical halt mechanism on every tier is real-time per-trade condition-code decoding (T1 resumption / T12 paused / LULD / regulatory) on the WS trade tape included in the tier we own. The real pre-live discharge is a BUILD on the tier we hold, not a buy on a higher tier. |
| **Problem** | `verify_halt_status` tick-dispatch is INTENTIONALLY UNWIRED (existing intentional gating at `supabase/functions/longshort-reconciliation-tick/index.ts:124`); `AlpacaHaltStatusFetcher` is structurally phantom (`/v2/assets/{symbol}.status` is static tradability metadata, not real-time halt state); under clause (s) halt status on paper is TYPED-ABSENT. That posture is NOT-LIVE-ACCEPTABLE per clause (s) SCOPE BOUNDARY — a real per-symbol `halted_at` / `resumed_at` event source must be wired into the live-order code path before live-fire ratification. Today the repo has ZERO Polygon WS infrastructure (no `WebSocket(` consumers in `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-broker/` against the Polygon real-time host) — both the wire AND the integration risk are unverified-in-repo. |
| **Fix Shape (BUILD on the tier we own; NOT a procurement)** | Two-step build: **Step A — WS PROBE FIRST (mandatory, literal-over-mental-model).** A one-shot read-only probe opens the Polygon Advanced WS `T.*` (trades) channel with the production `POLYGON_API_KEY_PROD` key, subscribes to a small symbol set spanning a known historical halt window (or a live RTH window with elevated halt activity), and records: (a) connection established on the tier we already own; (b) `T` messages flow in real-time; (c) halt-class condition codes (T1 resumption / T12 paused / LULD / regulatory) appear in the `c[]` (conditions) array for halted-name trades, with realistic latency vs the tape-gap; (d) the gap-replay surface (sequence numbers / `/v3/trades` REST backfill) works as documented. Probe VERDICT recorded BEFORE any fetcher code lands (probe-first gate). The probe is ACT-400-shaped (read-only, time-bounded, no production wiring) but WS-scoped rather than REST-scoped. **Step B — FETCHER BUILD.** WS subscriber on `T.*` (filtered to current universe) + condition-code halt-state decoder (maps `c[]` codes → `{halted: bool, halted_at?, resumed_at?, reason: 'T12'|'LULD'|'regulatory'|'resumption_T1'}`) + reconnect / gap-replay state machine + `/v3/trades` REST backfill / replay path for missed windows + idempotency (per-symbol event dedup) + tests against the live feed → real per-symbol `halted_at` / `resumed_at` events surfaced via a new `PolygonHaltStatusFetcher` injected at the `broker-bootstrap.ts` seam → `verify_halt_status` tick-dispatch wired NON-PHANTOM at `longshort-reconciliation-tick/index.ts:124` against the §11.0.7 #6 contract (strong tier, low_tolerance, skip-this-name-this-tick failure action). The existing `AlpacaHaltStatusFetcher` is REPLACED, not consumed. The clause-(s) GUARDRAIL 2 surfacing (`summary.halt_status_unavailable` / `names_placed_without_halt_check`) is wired AT THE SAME TIME so the typed-absence-on-paper diagnostic remains visible during the build's degraded-paths and the pre-cutover window. |
| **Why deferred (NOT bundled into this PR; NOT bundled into FP-062 closure)** | (1) Opportunity-cost sequencing: FP-062 gap (e) (first end-to-end live-fills validation) is the discrete-closure GATE for Phase 6 and is market-gated; halt-coverage on PAPER protects no real capital (clause (s) RATIONALE). Building FP-064 before gap-(e) discharges burns build-cycles on a safety control with zero paper-window ROI while the live-fills validation remains the binding gate. (2) Probe-first discipline: the vendor-doc claim that halt-class condition codes flow on the Advanced WS tier is NOT in-repo-verified — running Step A is a precondition for committing to Step B's scope. (3) Integration-risk amortization: building the first Polygon WS subscriber under live-money pressure compresses unrelated risks; deferring to AFTER gap-(e) discharge lets the WS infrastructure land with paper validation before live-fire ratification. The paper-window mitigation is clause (s) typed-absence + GUARDRAIL 2 operator-visible diagnostic + the signal-layer degraded-volume/spread defense (DW-067). |
| **Scheduling** | Build-mode opens at FP-062 gap-(e) discharge (first `longshort.execution.filled` event observed end-to-end with the ACT-403 fill-accounting path exercising correctly). Until then FP-064 is the named pre-live blocker per DEC-068 clause (s) GUARDRAIL 3. Step A (WS probe) is the first build action and is itself gated to record a probe verdict BEFORE Step B branches open. |
| **Acceptance Criteria (when build branch opens)** | (1) **Step A probe verdict** recorded as an ACT-XXX-shaped artifact BEFORE any Step B fetcher code lands (probe-first gate; mirrors the ACT-400 probe artifact shape). (2) **`PolygonHaltStatusFetcher`** wired at the `broker-bootstrap.ts` seam, replacing `AlpacaHaltStatusFetcher`; injection follows the existing `PolygonQuoteFetcher` injection precedent. (3) **`verify_halt_status`** tick-dispatch wired non-phantom at `longshort-reconciliation-tick/index.ts:124` (the existing intentional gating is REMOVED in the same commit that lands the wire); verifier behavior conforms to §11.0.7 #6 strong-tier / low-tolerance / skip-this-name-this-tick action. (4) **Clause (s) GUARDRAIL 2** surfacing wired at the placement / tick surface: response and audit metadata MUST include `summary.halt_status_unavailable: boolean` and `names_placed_without_halt_check: string[]`. (5) **Verification log** against the live feed: at least one real T12 (or T1 resumption / LULD halt) detected end-to-end during the build window, with `halted_at` / `resumed_at` events flowing into the verifier and the diagnostic surfacing observed. (6) **Gap-replay attestation**: a forced disconnect + reconnect during the verification window does not drop halt events (REST backfill closes the gap). (7) **Live-fire ratification DEC** cites FP-064 closure evidence (probe verdict + build attestation + verification log + gap-replay attestation) as a prerequisite. |
| **Constraints (STOP-conditions)** | Skipping Step A (WS probe) and going straight to Step B → STOP (literal-over-mental-model violation; the vendor-doc claim is unverified-in-repo). Framing FP-064 as a procurement / buy (vs a build on the tier we hold) → STOP (the whole point of this charter is correcting that framing). Wiring `verify_halt_status` non-phantom on PAPER before Step A verdict records that real halt-class condition codes actually flow on our tier → STOP (would create a phantom-real verifier worse than the typed-absent posture). Bundling FP-064 build into FP-062 closure → STOP (FP-062 closure gates on gap (e) live-fills; FP-064 is the LIVE-fire gate, not the Phase-6-closure gate). Removing the clause-(s) GUARDRAIL 2 surfacing once the real fetcher is wired → STOP (the surfacing must persist for any degraded path where the WS feed is down — typed-absence can recur transiently in production). Editing the ACT-400 DW-058-B2 addendum to "correct" it inline → STOP (preserved-record precedent per clause (p); the correction is APPENDED at DW-058-B2-ADD-02). |

### FP-065: Per-lot entry-attribution (full §1.1) — planner-seam rank/score capture + client_order_id ↔ attribution sidecar + order_id ↔ CID lineage (CHARTER STUB; MONEY-PATH; post-window; pre-authorized by operator at ACT-419)

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| **Status** | REGISTERED — build deferred to post-window. Authored 2026-06-30 at ACT-419 concurrent with the FP-062 ranking-snapshot sidecar ship (MIG-149 + `rebalance-ranking-snapshot-writer.ts`). |
| **Tier** | A — MONEY-PATH (touches the planner seam + introduces a CID sidecar + resolves the order_id ↔ client_order_id lineage hop that joins back to `longshort_lots.source_order_id`). |
| **Mode** | DOCUMENTATION-ONLY at registration. Build mode opens post-window (NOT against a market-clock deadline) so dual-investigation + lineage-hop verification get full rigor. |
| **Origin** | Operator-authorized Option 1 (snapshot-now + full-§1.1-after) on the ROI-attribution data-substrate gap analysis. The ACT-419 ranking-snapshot is the **batch-one floor** — it captures the rank/score state of the consumed generation via an INDEPENDENT read with skew-detection, so today's early fills are recoverable via post-hoc join on (operator_id, as_of_date, snapshot_computed_at, side, ticker). FP-065 is the **per-lot perfection** for batch 2 onward: rank/score/as_of/intraday_slot pinned at the exact planner decision, lineaged through CID, joined to `longshort_lots` by `source_order_id`. |
| **Problem** | Even with the ranking-snapshot landed, per-lot attribution is approximate: a single snapshot pins the rank for a (side, ticker) at fire-time, but cannot disambiguate which specific lot within a multi-fill order was attributed at which exact decision point, and cannot capture planner-derived sizing context (slot, sizing_basis, ranker-ref-quote). Recovery from the snapshot requires the join chain `longshort_lots.source_order_id → orders → client_order_id → snapshot row`, which depends on the order_id ↔ CID lineage being intact end-to-end. Today the planner does NOT emit a CID sidecar at decision-time; the CID is generated downstream at submit-time, so attribution context that exists at the planner seam is dropped before the CID is minted. |
| **Fix Shape (planner-seam capture + CID sidecar + lineage resolution)** | (1) At the planner seam (`rebalance-planner.ts`), capture per-decision attribution (`as_of_date`, `intraday_slot`, side, ticker, rank, score, ranker_source, `ranker_ref_quote`, sizing_basis) into a typed record threaded with each planned action. (2) At the submit seam, the CID minted for the order is recorded together with the planner attribution into a new sidecar table `longshort_planner_attribution` keyed on `(operator_id, client_order_id)`. (3) Lineage hop: confirm (or wire) that `longshort_lots.source_order_id` joins back to the broker order whose `client_order_id` is the sidecar key — settle whether the join is direct (`lots.source_order_id = orders.id`) or requires a `lots → fills → orders` two-hop, and document the canonical path. (4) Backfill posture: existing rows backfill via the snapshot (operator_id, as_of_date, side, ticker) approximate-join; FP-065 rows are perfect-attribution from the planner forward. |
| **Invariant Preserved (load-bearing)** | The ACT-419 ranking-snapshot's separation-purity invariant (`runRebalanceSubmit` signature byte-identical; reader pure; fire-and-forget) is NOT a constraint on FP-065 — FP-065 IS the sanctioned planner-seam touch, with the dual-investigation + post-window rigor the snapshot sidecar deliberately avoided. The snapshot keeps batch-one recoverable while FP-065 is built without time pressure. |
| **Why deferred (post-window)** | Three money-path coupling points: planner capture, CID sidecar, order_id ↔ CID lineage hop. Each requires its own investigation + verification; bundling against a ~3.5h pre-RTH clock would compress them. The snapshot sidecar bounds the cost of deferral: batch-one attribution is recoverable post-hoc via the snapshot, so FP-065 schedules into the calibration window without a hard deadline. |
| **Acceptance Criteria (when build branch opens)** | (1) Planner attribution record typed + captured at every planner decision in `rebalance-planner.ts`, with all fields enumerated in this charter. (2) `longshort_planner_attribution` table migration with full GRANTs + RLS + deny-write (mirrors MIG-149 template). (3) CID sidecar write wired at the submit seam IN THE SAME COMMIT as the lineage-hop resolution (no half-landed CID without lineage). (4) Lineage hop documented + asserted with a regression test that joins a synthetic `longshort_lot` → `longshort_planner_attribution` end-to-end. (5) Backfill SQL view documented that approximates pre-FP-065 lots via the ranking-snapshot join. (6) DEC-XXX ratifying the planner-attribution record shape (additive to RebalanceAction or equivalent). |
| **Constraints (STOP-conditions)** | Landing the CID sidecar write without the lineage-hop resolution in the same commit → STOP (creates a sidecar that cannot be joined back). Changing the order_id ↔ CID minting path to make lineage work → STOP if it touches the submitter without a separate DEC (this is a substrate-design change). Removing the ranking-snapshot once FP-065 lands → STOP (snapshot remains the canonical batch-one attribution + the skew-detection signal stays valuable as a cadence-tuning input). Bundling FP-065 build into FP-062 closure → STOP (FP-062 gap (e) is live-fills validation; FP-065 is the per-lot attribution perfection, separate gate). |
| **Cross-references** | ACT-419 (this FP's registration action + the ranking-snapshot sidecar ship that bounds its deferral cost); MIG-149 (`longshort_rebalance_ranking_snapshot` — the batch-one attribution floor this FP perfects); `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-execution/rebalance-ranking-snapshot-writer.ts` (the independent-read writer the FP-065 planner-seam capture supersedes for batch 2 onward); `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-execution/rebalance-planner.ts` (the planner seam where attribution is captured); `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-execution/rebalance-submit-orchestrator.ts:193-217` (the reader pattern the snapshot mirrors; planner-attribution will be additive to the planner, not the reader); `longshort_lots.source_order_id` (the join target for lineage resolution); DEC-070 clause (a) `intraday_slot` PK rotation (the slot field the planner capture must include); ROI-attribution data-substrate gap analysis turn (the §1.1 seed entry this FP discharges); FP-062 (the Phase-6 integration this FP is sequenced AFTER). |

## FP-066 — DW-168 Pre-Buildable Sanity-Probe Shelf (Lambdarank Sanity Gate, Read-Only)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Status** | **CHARTERED 2026-06-30 at ACT-423 — charter only, NO probe code this turn.** Independent Lovable scoping per operator brief; supervisor charter reconciled separately. Authorized by DW-168-ADD-01 disposition-reopen (ACT-422) which moved DW-168 from "DATA-GATED — no code now" → "PARTIALLY DATA-GATED" and named P1/P2/P3/P4 as the pre-buildable shelf. Sub-step build authorizations are SEPARATE later turns. |
| **Tier** | A — DW-168 is the promotion-trust gate on the critical path to first combiner candidate promotion (DEC-070 h.5). |
| **Authority anchors** | DW-168 (original + ADD-01 at ACT-422); DW-205 (contingent trainer-slot-awareness sibling — informed, NOT discharged, by this FP's probes); DEC-070 clause (h).2 (the by-design daily-T+H label shared across slots invariant NEW-1 measures); DEC-076 clause (a) (the v1-now / supersede-on-first-run pattern S3 mirrors); FP-064 charter at ACT-416 (the CHARTERED-CONTINGENT precedent S4 mirrors); CROSSWIND §6.1/§6.2 (NDCG@25 / lambdarank objective the harness extends against, no objective edit). |
| **Problem** | Pre-first-candidate, the only DW-168 signal we have is observational substrate evidence. Three risks crystallize without the shelf: (i) NEW-1 — within-group label-tie ratio (lambdarank's pairwise loss collapses to uninformative when most pairs share the daily T+H label, the DEC-070 h.2 by-design consequence); (ii) NEW-2 — feature variation across `intraday_slot` (if features are slot-invariant, the slot-multiplied rows are pure label-duplication, not new signal); (iii) group-cardinality drift across rotation (per-`seed_as_of_date` group sizes with/without slot grouping inform the DW-205 grouping-strategy decision). Today the trainer is slot-blind (groups by `seed_as_of_date` alone, `trainer.py:268+`); the probes measure the consequences the trainer cannot see, BEFORE the first late-July candidate trains. |
| **Goal** | Land a READ-ONLY observational probe shelf + harness skeleton that (a) emits early-warning evidence on NEW-1/NEW-2/cardinality from substrate accruing NOW, (b) pre-stages the NDCG-delta + ε-tolerance harness so the first candidate evaluation is not blocked on tooling, (c) governs FAIL responses via a pre-named investigation FP skeleton. Zero trainer touch, zero candidate fabrication, zero money-path write. |
| **Build order (value × readiness, independently scoped)** | **WAVE-1 BUILD-FIRST (substrate accruing NOW — highest early-warning value):** S1a NEW-1 label-tie-ratio probe; S1b group-cardinality histogram (slot vs no-slot); S1c NEW-2 feature-variation probe. **WAVE-2 RIDE-ALONG (governance, low cost, blocks nothing but tightens the gate):** S3 ε-tolerance DEC (mirrors DEC-076 a — v1-now, supersede-on-first-run). **WAVE-3 DEFER-UNTIL-CANDIDATE-IMMINENT (no input until ~late-July candidate):** S2 NDCG-delta harness extension of `promotion-criteria-evaluator.ts:146` ndcgAtK — chartered, scaffolded as a no-arg skeleton, NOT exercised until the first candidate row exists in `combiner_model_registry`. **WAVE-4 CHARTERED-CONTINGENT (FP-064 precedent):** S4 FAIL-investigation FP skeleton, activated only on a declared FAIL from the first DW-168 evaluation; three pre-named arms map to the original DW-168 scope (e) (i)/(ii)/(iii). Rationale for the ordering: S1a-c read REAL substrate today; S2 has no candidate to feed; S3 is governance not code; S4 is a skeleton not a build. |
| **Sub-step S1a — NEW-1 label-tie-ratio probe (READ-ONLY)** | **Read:** `combiner_forward_returns(seed_as_of_date, intraday_slot, horizon_td, ticker, side, side_signed_return, price_source_status)` filtered to `source_table='combiner_book'`, `price_source_status='success'`, `horizon_td=10` (the §6.1/§6.2-locked training horizon). **Group:** per `(seed_as_of_date, intraday_slot)` AND per `seed_as_of_date` (both groupings emitted side-by-side to inform DW-205). **Compute:** within each group, the count of ordered pairs `(i,j)` with `i<j` where `side_signed_return[i] == side_signed_return[j]` divided by `n*(n-1)/2`. Tie tolerance = exact float equality first; emit ε-tolerant variant (ε=1e-9) as a sibling column for sensitivity. **Emit:** stdout JSONL: `{date, slot|null, n, tied_pairs, total_pairs, tie_ratio, tie_ratio_eps}`. **Early-warning interpretation:** `tie_ratio > 0.20` averaged over the trailing 10 sessions ⇒ flag DW-168-PRE-WARN-NEW1; `> 0.50` ⇒ flag DW-205 BLOCKING (the slot-merged grouping is producing predominantly uninformative pairs; trainer change becomes pre-candidate priority). |
| **Sub-step S1b — group-cardinality histogram (READ-ONLY)** | **Read:** same query as S1a (cardinalities derive from the same rows). **Compute:** per-`seed_as_of_date` group sizes under two groupings: (i) `group_by(seed_as_of_date)` — the trainer's current grouping; (ii) `group_by(seed_as_of_date, intraday_slot)` — the slot-aware alternative DW-205 contemplates. Emit count-distribution (min/p25/median/p75/max + histogram bins {<10, 10-25, 25-50, 50-100, 100-250, >250}). **Early-warning interpretation:** slot-aware groups predominantly `<25` (the NDCG@25 cutoff) ⇒ flag DW-168-PRE-WARN-CARDINALITY; the slot-aware path would shrink groups below the ranking cutoff and the slot-merged path remains defensible per DEC-070 h.2. Symmetric reading: slot-merged groups predominantly `>250` ⇒ confirms the merge is harvesting cross-slot pair density the slot-aware path would forfeit. |
| **Sub-step S1c — NEW-2 feature-variation probe (READ-ONLY)** | **Read:** `combiner_feature_vectors(seed_as_of_date, intraday_slot, ticker, features jsonb)` for the last N=10 sessions where ≥2 distinct `intraday_slot` values exist per `(seed_as_of_date, ticker)`. **Compute:** per `(date, ticker)`, materialize feature rows in `FEATURE_ORDER` (the 18-key contract — imported from `training/combiner/feature_contract.py` to avoid drift); compute pairwise L2 distance across the slot-row matrix; emit the distance distribution + the per-feature variance ratio `Var(feature across slots) / Var(feature across tickers within same slot)`. **Early-warning interpretation:** median pairwise L2 < 0.05 across the 18-key vector ⇒ flag DW-168-PRE-WARN-NEW2 (intraday rows are near-duplicate of EOD; the slot rotation may be substrate-noise rather than signal — this is the load-bearing input to DW-205's `decision-pending-evidence` resolution). Per-feature variance ratio < 0.10 on ≥12/18 keys ⇒ ESCALATE to DW-205-BLOCKING-NEW2 (slot rotation contributes no meaningful feature signal). |
| **Sub-step S2 — NDCG-delta harness skeleton (SCAFFOLDED, NOT EXERCISED)** | **Lands:** TS module at `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-combiner/dw168-ndcg-delta-harness.ts` exporting `ndcgDelta(candidateScores, baselineScores, labels, k=25)` that wraps the existing `ndcgAtK` at `promotion-criteria-evaluator.ts:146` (no edit to the existing function — wrapper only). **Why scaffolded now:** the API surface + ε-tolerance application point are pre-frozen so the first candidate evaluation is not blocked on harness authoring. **Why NOT exercised now:** zero rows in `combiner_model_registry` with `status='candidate'`; running against synthetic data would fabricate a candidate (STOP-condition). **Exercise trigger:** first real candidate insertion via `trainer.py`. |
| **Sub-step S3 — ε-tolerance DEC (governance, NO CODE)** | **Authors:** DEC-077 (next free decision id — grep-confirm at authoring turn) ratifying the ε-tolerance value applied to NDCG-delta + tie-ratio computations under DEC-076 a's v1-now / supersede-on-first-run pattern. **v1 values:** ε_ndcg = 1e-6 (matches the order of magnitude of typical NDCG@25 differences observed in lambdarank literature); ε_tie = 1e-9 (float-rounding floor for `side_signed_return` equality). **Supersession rule:** the first DW-168 evaluation run that produces a candidate with NDCG-delta within [−2·ε_ndcg, +2·ε_ndcg] of the live baseline triggers a DEC-077-ADD-01 ε re-ratification turn. |
| **Sub-step S4 — FAIL-investigation FP skeleton (CHARTERED-CONTINGENT)** | **Pattern:** mirrors FP-064 (ACT-416) — chartered now, START gated on a named trigger (FAIL declaration on first DW-168 evaluation OR any DW-168-PRE-WARN ESCALATION from S1a/S1c). **Three pre-named arms** (map to original DW-168 scope (e)): (α) trainer-grouping fix arm — applies the DW-205 slot-aware grouping if S1b cardinality permits; (β) label-construction fix arm — revisits `_labels_to_relevance` at `trainer.py:278` (quantile-bin re-tune within §6.2's surrogate envelope); (γ) feature-set fix arm — adds slot-discriminating features under a §6.5 / DEC-066 supersession DEC. Skeleton names the arms; each arm becomes its own FP at activation. |
| **Where it lands + cadence** | **S1a/S1b/S1c:** Python under `training/combiner/scripts/dw168_probes/` (sibling to `assert_feature_order_parity.py`); reuses `feature_contract.FEATURE_ORDER` import + `supabase` python client (already in `requirements.in`). Each probe is its own `__main__`-runnable file emitting JSONL to stdout — composes cleanly with GHA log capture and with operator-local invocation. **No edge function, no cron in v1:** weekly GHA workflow `dw168-probes.yml` (sibling to `combiner-train.yml`) runs Mon 06:00 UTC (after weekend's accrual settles and before the Sunday-night training window of §6.3); workflow uploads JSONL artifacts and posts a one-line summary to the action log. Rationale: the probes are observational not money-path; an edge-fn surface would be over-engineering for a read-only weekly observability emit, and the GHA path piggybacks on the already-provisioned `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` secret (DEC-064). **S2:** TS shared module (in-substrate); no cron, called by the post-training evaluator at first-candidate. **S3:** governance markdown only. **S4:** governance markdown only until activation. |
| **Read-only invariants (STOP-conditions, must hold across every sub-step)** | (R1) Probes read `combiner_feature_vectors` + `combiner_forward_returns` ONLY; any read of a money-path table (`longshort_lots`, `longshort_target_positions`, `combiner_book`, `combiner_rankings`, `kill_switches`, etc.) → STOP. (R2) Zero writes to ANY table; the probes emit JSONL to stdout / GHA artifacts only — no `INSERT`, no `UPDATE`, no Storage write. (R3) Zero edit to `trainer.py`, `promotion-criteria-evaluator.ts:146` ndcgAtK, or any `lgbm-inference.ts` surface — S2 wraps, never patches. (R4) Zero candidate fabrication: no synthetic `combiner_model_registry` row, no synthetic ranking, no test that asserts against a fabricated training run. (R5) DEC-070 clause (h).2 by-design slot-merge is NOT a defect to fix in this FP — DW-205 remains decision-pending-evidence; this FP supplies the evidence, it does not act on it. |
| **Acceptance Criteria (when sub-step build branches open)** | (1) Each WAVE-1 probe emits stable JSONL with the fields enumerated above on a real substrate read against the live project, with a fixture-mode `--dry-run` flag for sandbox-only invocation. (2) Each probe has a pytest under `training/combiner/tests/` asserting the field set + the early-warning threshold logic against a small synthetic substrate fixture (the fixture IS NOT a candidate model — it is raw `combiner_*` rows). (3) S2 harness skeleton lands with a vitest asserting the `ndcgDelta` API surface against known-NDCG fixtures (no candidate involved). (4) S3 lands as DEC-077 with v1 ε values + supersession rule. (5) S4 lands as a charter-only entry in `feature-proposals.md` with the three arms enumerated, status `CHARTERED-CONTINGENT`. (6) `dw168-probes.yml` GHA workflow lands with weekly schedule + manual `workflow_dispatch` + artifact upload — no secret beyond the existing service-role key. (7) DW-168-ADD-02 disposition update logged when the first WAVE-1 emission produces interpretable evidence (PASS / PRE-WARN / ESCALATE). |
| **Constraints (STOP-conditions, FP-level)** | Building S2 against a synthetic candidate to "validate the harness end-to-end" → STOP; the harness is API-shape-validated only, the end-to-end exercise is the first real candidate. Bundling S4 activation into this FP → STOP; S4 stays a skeleton until a named trigger. Promoting any probe finding to a trainer change inside this FP → STOP; trainer changes flow through DW-205 + an arm-specific FP under S4. Authoring an edge-fn surface for the probes → STOP unless a later operator turn explicitly ratifies the cadence change from weekly-GHA → in-substrate. Reading any money-path table from a probe → STOP (R1). Writing to ANY table from a probe → STOP (R2). |
| **Cross-references** | ACT-422 (DW-168-ADD-01 disposition-reopen + P1-P4 + NEW-1/NEW-2 authorization this FP discharges); ACT-423 (this FP's registration action); DW-168 (the governance entry — original + ADD-01); DW-205 (the contingent sibling this FP supplies evidence for, does NOT discharge); DEC-070 clause (h).2 (the by-design slot-merge invariant NEW-1 measures); DEC-076 clause (a) (the v1-now / supersede-on-first-run pattern S3 mirrors); FP-064 charter at ACT-416 (the CHARTERED-CONTINGENT precedent S4 mirrors); `combiner_feature_vectors` + `combiner_forward_returns` schemas at HEAD `3eaa7757` (MIG-115 horizon widen + MIG 20260626191013/191106 slot rotation — the substrate the probes read); `training/combiner/trainer.py:268+` (the slot-blind grouping the probes measure the consequences of) and `:278` `_labels_to_relevance` (NEW-1's tied-label construction seam); `training/combiner/feature_contract.py` `FEATURE_ORDER` (the 18-key vector NEW-2 walks); `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-combiner/promotion-criteria-evaluator.ts:146` `ndcgAtK` (the harness S2 wraps); `.github/workflows/combiner-train.yml` (the sibling-workflow template `dw168-probes.yml` mirrors); ACT-335 / ACT-410 (the late-July first-candidate ETA gating S2 exercise + S4 activation); Constitution Rule 8 (charter additive — DW-168 + DW-205 original fields PRESERVED at their entries). |

## FP-067 — Per-Signal × Per-Side Return Decomposition Capture (Register Item 2.1)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Status** | **W1 CLOSED 2026-07-01 at ACT-433** (Option A ratified via FP-067-ADD-02; MIG-150 applied — `side` first-class + PK rotated 4→5-tuple; fallback per-signal decomposition sidecar wired at the ranker-orchestrator compose seam, fire-and-forget). CHARTERED 2026-06-30 at ACT-431. **W2 (LGBM SHAP writer) PENDING** — activates on first `promote_combiner_model` two-side flip. **W3 (FP-058 read-view) PENDING** — activates under FP-058. |
| **Tier** | A — 2.1 is a time-foreclosing ROI-attribution substrate capture; every filled day without decomposition capture on the LGBM-promoted path forecloses that day's per-signal attribution. Nuance: on the CURRENT fallback path the foreclosure is far weaker than the register framing implies (see "Cheap-now window" below). |
| **Authority anchors** | ROI-attribution register `docs/06-tracking/roi-attribution-substrate-register.md` item **2.1** (the GAP entry this FP discharges); DW-109 + DW-109-ADD-01 at ACT-430 (the gate-ablation read-out that established per-signal ablation as the NEXT open ROI question); FP-058 (the Phase-7 per-signal-ablation umbrella this capture feeds); DEC-066 (the 18-feature contract the decomposition walks); DEC-070 clause (a) `intraday_slot` PK rotation (the slot-key rotation any new capture MUST include, per the 1.1 keystone precedent); DEC-070 clause (h).2 (the substrate-additive-not-trainer-change pattern); DEC-074 (the `SIGNAL_IDS_FALLBACK_SUM` catalog-order determinism the fallback decomposition inherits); DEC-063 clause C6 SHAP-traceable (temporally-carved but the surface `combiner_shap_attribution` was scoped there — 2.1 is the writer this FP would land). |
| **The problem restated** | `combiner_forward_returns.seed_score` (`supabase/migrations/20260619014910…:67`) persists the COLLAPSED composite. Post-hoc reconstruction of "signal #k's per-side contribution to that seed_score" is: (i) EXACT + FREE today on the fallback path via `combiner_feature_vectors.features` × ranker formula; (ii) FORECLOSED once LGBM promotes (composite becomes a tree-path sum, not a linear feature × uniform-weight). FP-058 ("which signals earn their keep per side") depends on this decomposition being present in the substrate at query time. |
| **Independent decomposition finding (grounded)** | The fallback composite is **linearly separable per included signal**: `contribution_i = value_i / max(1, presentCount)` for every present signal (critical or non-critical), with critical-null-gated skips per DEC-071 sub-step 3c. Grounded at `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-combiner/ranker.ts:94-171` (the `computeComposite` loop) + `ranker.ts:13` docblock. **Per-side is trivially derivable:** `short_score = -composite` (register 2.2 / `ranker.ts:228`) → per-signal per-side contribution is a sign flip of the long-side contribution. **Consequence:** during the fallback era, per-signal decomposition is losslessly reconstructible from `combiner_feature_vectors.features` + `gated_signals` joined to `combiner_rankings` on (op, as_of_date, ticker, intraday_slot). **No new capture is required for fallback.** The foreclosure bites at LGBM promotion (per DEC-070 h.5, late-July 2026 first-candidate ETA per ACT-335 / ACT-410), when raw-feature → composite becomes a non-linear tree-path attribution requiring SHAP-per-row capture. |
| **Independent target-surface finding (grounded)** | An attribution surface **already exists structurally**: `public.combiner_shap_attribution` (`supabase/migrations/20260616103102…:205-217`), shape `(operator_id, as_of_date, ticker, attributions jsonb, model_id, computed_at)`, FK CASCADE to `combiner_rankings`, RESTRICTIVE deny-write RLS on authenticated (service_role writer only). **Three defects vs 2.1 needs, requiring an additive migration BEFORE any writer lands:** (D1) **NOT slot-rotated** — PK is `(op, as_of, ticker)` while `combiner_rankings` PK was rotated to `(op, as_of, ticker, intraday_slot)` at MIG-122 (`supabase/migrations/20260626190957…`); the FK on `combiner_shap_attribution` references a bare triple that is no longer unique on `combiner_rankings`; the FK is stale — this is the exact §1.1-keystone foreclosure repeated on the attribution surface. (D2) **No writer** — grep confirms `combiner_shap_attribution` has zero producers across `supabase/functions/**`; the SHAP capture path was carved out under DEC-063 clause C6. (D3) **No `side` column** — the `attributions jsonb` blob can carry per-side sub-objects, but a first-class `side` column would let a cheap-view (register 2.2) join per-side per-signal to `combiner_forward_returns.side_signed_return` without unnesting. Charter decision: **EXTEND the existing surface** (rotate PK + fix FK + add `side` if the shape argument survives review) rather than author a new parallel table — a parallel table would fork the attribution namespace and violate the "one substrate per question" pattern of the register. |
| **Write-seam finding (grounded)** | The compose seam lives at `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-combiner/ranker-orchestrator.ts` — the same orchestrator that UPSERTs `combiner_rankings`. **Two candidate seams:** (S-inline) inline in the orchestrator UPSERT loop, single transaction with the ranking write — strongest consistency but couples attribution failure to money-path failure (violates the ACT-419 sidecar precedent). (S-sidecar) fire-and-forget writer analogous to `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-execution/rebalance-ranking-snapshot-writer.ts` at ACT-419 — attribution failure NEVER blocks the ranking write. **Charter decision: S-sidecar** — the attribution capture is diagnostic substrate, NOT money-path; the ACT-419 precedent is load-bearing for the "attribution never breaks trading" property this register exists to preserve. **Idempotency shape:** PK `(op, as_of_date, ticker, intraday_slot)` UPSERT with `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE SET attributions = EXCLUDED.attributions, computed_at = EXCLUDED.computed_at` — mirrors the `combiner_rankings` UPSERT pattern; slot-rotation-safe because slot is in PK. |
| **Forward-return join finding (grounded)** | `combiner_forward_returns` PK is `(op, as_of_date, ticker, intraday_slot, horizon_days)` per MIG 20260626191106; `side_signed_return` is populated per-horizon per-side (the register-1.2 substrate). Capturing attribution keyed by `(op, as_of_date, ticker, intraday_slot)` — the SAME quadruple minus horizon — makes the join a natural left-join on the quadruple + a per-side unnest, joining any per-signal contribution to any horizon's `side_signed_return` without additional keys. **Charter conclusion:** the quadruple-key + per-side split is sufficient join surface for the FP-058 ablation; nothing in the join argument justifies adding a `horizon` column to the attribution capture (attribution is horizon-agnostic; realized-return is horizon-typed — the join carries the horizon). |
| **Cheap-now-window finding (Charter's load-bearing scope trim)** | On the current fallback path per-signal contribution = `features[signal_id] / presentCount` (present signals only, gated-nulls skipped). This is **EXACTLY reconstructible today** from `combiner_feature_vectors` — a table that MIG-122 sibling migration MIG-123 rotated to `(op, as_of, ticker, intraday_slot)` PK — so slot-rotation does NOT foreclose fallback-era decomposition. **The urgency framing in register 2.1 ("every fill without capture is lost evidence") is CORRECT for the LGBM-promoted era and OVER-STATED for the fallback era.** Consequence for build-order (§Sub-steps below): the LGBM writer (WAVE-2) is the ROI-critical unit; the fallback writer (WAVE-1) is a CORRECTNESS-of-substrate-shape investment that de-risks WAVE-2, not a foreclosure fix in itself. |
| **Sub-step W1 — Extend surface + fallback-path writer (BUILD-AUTHORIZED-CONTINGENT-ON-DUAL-INVESTIGATION)** | (a) Migration MIG-{next-free}: rotate `combiner_shap_attribution` PK to `(op, as_of_date, ticker, intraday_slot)`; drop + re-create the FK to `combiner_rankings` on the rotated quadruple; add `side text NOT NULL CHECK (side IN ('long','short','both'))` — `'both'` is the fallback-path capture shape (contributions are unsigned + long/short symmetric via `-composite`); LGBM path will emit two rows per (quadruple), side='long' + side='short'. (b) `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-combiner/attribution-decomposition-writer.ts` — pure module computing `{signal_id → contribution_to_composite}` per row from `computeComposite` internals + returning the `attributions` jsonb blob; unit-tested against the same fixtures as `ranker_test.ts`. (c) sidecar hook in `ranker-orchestrator.ts` post-`combiner_rankings` UPSERT — fire-and-forget promise chain, structured logging on failure, NEVER throws into the orchestrator's return. (d) reference-index updates in `docs/07-reference/function-index.md` + `docs/07-reference/database-migration-ledger.md`. (e) replay-determinism test: capture-writer output must be byte-identical across re-runs (feeds off the same catalog-order iteration `SIGNAL_IDS_FALLBACK_SUM` DEC-074 the ranker uses). |
| **Sub-step W2 — LGBM-path writer (CHARTERED-CONTINGENT — activated on first LGBM promotion)** | Trigger: the first `promote_combiner_model` execution that flips `combiner_model_registry.status='active'` on both sides. (a) SHAP computation via `lgbm-inference.ts`-adjacent `treeShap` (tree-path attribution; LightGBM text-dump inference already lands the raw tree structure at `parseLgbmTreeDump` in `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-combiner/lgbm-inference.ts`, per DEC-065). (b) emit TWO rows per quadruple (side='long', side='short') carrying the per-side SHAP-per-signal jsonb. (c) `model_id` column repurposed as the write-time model attribution (already exists on the table). (d) tests: SHAP contributions sum-to-margin invariant per row + parity against LightGBM's `predict(pred_contrib=True)` on a fixture. (e) NO gate flip, NO promotion-criteria touch — this is capture-only per the "attribution never breaks trading" property. |
| **Sub-step W3 — FP-058 read-view (CHARTERED-DEFERRED)** | Governance-only skeleton for the FP-058 per-signal ablation view: SQL/materialized-view spec joining `combiner_shap_attribution` × `combiner_forward_returns` on the quadruple, aggregating per (signal_id, side, horizon) → mean/SE of `contribution_i × side_signed_return`. Lands under FP-058 when FP-058 activates; this FP registers the substrate shape only. |
| **Where it lands + cadence** | W1: additive migration + TS module + sidecar wire — same pattern as ACT-419 rebalance-ranking-snapshot-writer. W2: TS-only (extends existing lgbm-inference module) + migration only if the model_id FK needs re-scoping. W3: governance markdown only until FP-058 activates. NO cron changes across any wave — the writer piggybacks on the existing ranker cadence (main tick + shadow tick + manual). |
| **Read-only-until-approval invariants (STOP-conditions across every sub-step)** | (R1) Capture writer NEVER writes to `combiner_rankings`, `combiner_book`, `longshort_lots`, `longshort_target_positions`, `kill_switches` — attribution surface only. (R2) Capture writer NEVER blocks the money-path — fire-and-forget with structured logging on failure (ACT-419 sidecar pattern). (R3) Zero edit to `ranker.ts` pure layer — attribution derivation is a NEW pure module consuming the same `FeatureVectorRow` type; the ranker's composite formula is not changed. (R4) Zero edit to `promotion-criteria-evaluator.ts` — attribution is not a promotion gate. (R5) Zero flip of DEC-063 C6 carve-out — this FP LANDS the SHAP writer C6 anticipated, it does NOT ratify the traceability requirement earlier than DEC-063's deferral schedule permits. |
| **Acceptance Criteria (when sub-step build branches open)** | (1) W1 migration re-rotates `combiner_shap_attribution` PK, re-scopes FK, adds `side` column, preserves the RESTRICTIVE deny-write RLS; migration is idempotent (IF NOT EXISTS + guarded ALTER). (2) W1 writer emits one row per included (op, as_of, ticker, slot) with side='both' on the fallback path, `attributions` jsonb = `{signal_id: contribution}` map for every present signal, gated signals absent from the map; unit test asserts `sum(contributions) ≈ composite` within 1e-9 for a 100-vector fixture. (3) W1 sidecar has a failure-injection test proving orchestrator return payload is UNCHANGED when writer throws.
